\mathcal{T}\not\models s^{\prime}\sqsubseteq r
. Then, by definition of

\mathcal{J}
,
((a_{0},i),(b,j))\in s^{\prime\mathcal{J}}
since

((a_{0},i^{\prime}),(b,j^{\prime}))\in s^{\prime\mathcal{J}}
for all
i^{\prime},j^{\prime}\in\{1,2\}
.

•

\mathcal{T}\models s^{\prime}\sqsubseteq r
. Then
\mathcal{T}\models s\sqsubseteq r
. Note that, by construction of
\mathcal{I}
,
{\sf sig}(s)\cap\Sigma_{\mathsf{C}}=\emptyset
and
{\sf sig}(s^{\prime})\cap\Sigma_{\mathsf{C}}=\emptyset
. Hence, by construction of
\mathcal{J}
,
(i,j)=(1,2)
or
(i,j)=(2,1)
. In both cases we have

((a_{0},i),(b,j))\in s^{\prime\mathcal{J}}
as well.

To prove that all CIs of
\mathcal{T}
are satisfied in
\mathcal{J}

observe that
B^{\mathcal{J}}=(B^{\mathcal{I}}\times\{1\})\cup(B^{\mathcal{I}}\times\{2\})
holds for all
basic concepts
B
. Thus,
\mathcal{J}
satisfies all CIs satisfied in

\mathcal{I}
and, therefore, is a model of any CI in
\mathcal{T}
, as required.

It follows from Claims 1 and 2 that
(\mathcal{T},\Sigma_{\mathsf{C}})
is not convex for tCQs. The coNP-hardness of
tCQ evaluation follows from Lemma
8
. This finishes the proof of Point (1).

We come to the proof of Point (2). The
proof relies on the canonical model associated with an ABox
and a
\text{DL-Lite}_{\mathcal{R}}
TBox
[
20
,
37
]
.
Specifically, for every ABox
\mathcal{A}
that is consistent w.r.t. a
\text{DL-Lite}_{\mathcal{R}}
TBox
\mathcal{T}
without closed predicates, there is a model

\mathcal{I}
of
\mathcal{A}
and
\mathcal{T}
which is minimal in the sense that for all UCQs
q
and tuples

\vec{a}
in
{\sf Ind}(\mathcal{A})
:

\mathcal{A}\models(\mathcal{T},\emptyset,q)(\vec{a})\quad\text{iff}\quad\mathcal{I}\models q(\vec{a}).

We show that if
\mathcal{I}
is constructed in a careful way and

(\mathcal{T},\Sigma_{\mathsf{C}})
is safe, then
\mathcal{I}
respects
\Sigma_{\mathsf{C}}
. This means
that a tuple
\vec{a}\in\mathsf{Ind}(\mathcal{A})
is a certain answer to

(\mathcal{T},\emptyset,q)
on
\mathcal{A}
iff it is
a certain answer to
(\mathcal{T},\Sigma_{\mathsf{C}},q)
on
\mathcal{A}
since
closed predicates can only result in additional answers, but not in invalidating answers. It follows that
(\mathcal{T},\Sigma_{\mathsf{C}})
is
convex for tCQs and that
(\mathcal{T},\Sigma_{\mathsf{C}})
and

(\mathcal{T},\emptyset)
are UCQ-inseparable on consistent ABoxes (Point (a)).
Additionally, we show that ABox consistency w.r.t.
(\mathcal{T},\Sigma_{\mathsf{C}})
is FO-rewritable when

(\mathcal{T},\Sigma_{\mathsf{C}})
is safe, which together with the first observation implies that UCQ evaluation w.r.t. a safe
(\mathcal{T},\Sigma_{\mathsf{C}})
is FO-rewritable:
if
p_{c}
is an FO-rewriting of ABox consistency w.r.t.
(\mathcal{T},\Sigma_{\mathsf{C}})
and
q^{\prime}(\vec{x})
is an
FO-rewriting of

(\mathcal{T},\emptyset,q(\vec{x}))
, then
\neg p_{c}\vee q^{\prime}(\vec{x})
is an FO-rewriting

(\mathcal{T},\Sigma_{\mathsf{C}},q(\vec{x}))
.
Thus, Point (b) follows. It thus remains to prove Claims 3 and 4 below.

Claim 12
.

Let
(\mathcal{T},\Sigma_{\mathsf{C}})
be a safe
\text{DL-Lite}_{\mathcal{R}}
TBox with closed
predicates. Then for every UCQ
q
, we have

\mathcal{A}\models(\mathcal{T},\Sigma_{\mathsf{C}},q)(\vec{a})\quad\text{ iff
}\quad\mathcal{A}\models(\mathcal{T},\emptyset,q)(\vec{a})

for all ABoxes
\mathcal{A}
that are consistent w.r.t.
(\mathcal{T},\Sigma_{\mathsf{C}})
and
all
\vec{a}\in\mathsf{Ind}(\mathcal{A})
.

Proof 5.5

(Proof of claim.)
.

Let
(\mathcal{T},\Sigma_{\mathsf{C}})
be safe and assume that
\mathcal{A}
is consistent
w.r.t.
(\mathcal{T},\Sigma_{\mathsf{C}})
. We construct a canonical model of
\mathcal{A}
and
\mathcal{T}

as the interpretation corresponding to a (possibly infinite) ABox
\mathcal{A}_{c}
that is the limit
of a sequence of ABoxes
\mathcal{A}_{0},\mathcal{A}_{1},\ldots
. Let

\mathcal{A}_{0}=\mathcal{A}
and assume
\mathcal{A}_{j}
has been defined already. Then
\mathcal{A}_{j+1}
is obtained from
\mathcal{A}_{j}
by applying
the following two rules:

(R1)

if there exist roles
r,s
and
a,b\in{\sf N_{I}}
with
\mathcal{T}\models r\sqsubseteq s
,
r(a,b)\in\mathcal{A}_{j}
, and
s(a,b)\not\in\mathcal{A}_{j}
,
then add
s(a,b)
to
\mathcal{A}_{j}
;

(R2)

if (R1) does not apply and there are basic concepts
B_{1},B_{2}
and
a\in{\sf N_{I}}
such that
\mathcal{T}\models B_{1}\sqsubseteq B_{2}
,

a\in B_{1}^{\mathcal{I}_{\mathcal{A}_{j}}}
, and
a\not\in B_{2}^{\mathcal{I}_{\mathcal{A}_{j}}}
, then add
B_{2}(a)
to
\mathcal{A}_{j}
if
B_{2}
is a concept name
and add
r(a,b)
for some fresh
b\in{\sf N_{I}}
to
\mathcal{A}_{j}
if
B_{2}=\exists r
for some role
r
.

We assume that (R1) and (R2) are applied in a fair way. Now let
\mathcal{I}_{\mathcal{T},\mathcal{A}}=\mathcal{I}_{\mathcal{A}_{c}}
, where
\mathcal{A}_{c}=\bigcup_{i\geq 0}\mathcal{A}_{i}
.
It is known
[
37
]
(and easy to prove) that
\mathcal{I}_{\mathcal{T},\mathcal{A}}
is a model of
\mathcal{T}
and

\mathcal{A}
with the following properties:

(p1)

For all UCQs
q(\vec{x})
and
\vec{a}\in{\sf Ind}(\mathcal{A})
:
\mathcal{A}\models(\mathcal{T},\emptyset,q)(\vec{a})
iff
\mathcal{I}_{\mathcal{T},\mathcal{A}}\models q(\vec{a})
.

(p2)

For any individual name
b\in{\sf Ind}(\mathcal{A}_{c})\setminus{\sf Ind}(\mathcal{A})
introduced as a witness
for Rule (R2) for some CI of the form
B_{1}\sqsubseteq\exists s
and every basic concept
B

:
b\in B^{\mathcal{I}_{\mathcal{T},\mathcal{A}}}
iff
\mathcal{T}\models\exists s^{-}\sqsubseteq B
.

To show that
\mathcal{I}_{\mathcal{T},\mathcal{A}}
is a model of
\mathcal{T}
and
\mathcal{A}
that respects

\Sigma_{\mathsf{C}}
it is sufficient to prove that every assertion using
predicates from
\Sigma_{\mathsf{C}}
in
\mathcal{A}_{c}
is contained in
\mathcal{A}
. We first show that for all
a,b\in{\sf Ind}(\mathcal{A})
,

•

if
A(a)\in\mathcal{A}_{c}
and
A\in\Sigma_{\mathsf{C}}
, then

A(a)\in\mathcal{A}
; and

•

if
r(a,b)\in\mathcal{A}_{c}
and
r\in\Sigma_{\mathsf{C}}
,
then
r(a,b)\in\mathcal{A}
.

For a proof by contradiction assume that
A(a)\in\mathcal{A}_{c}
but

A(a)\not\in\mathcal{A}
for some concept name
A\in\Sigma_{\mathsf{C}}
.
By Point (p1), the former implies

\mathcal{A}\models(\mathcal{T},\emptyset,A(x))(a)
. Thus,
\mathcal{A}\models(\mathcal{T},\Sigma_{\mathsf{C}},A(x))(a)
which contradicts the assumption that

\mathcal{A}
is consistent w.r.t.
(\mathcal{T},\Sigma_{\mathsf{C}})
. The argument
for role assertions
r(a,b)
is similar and omitted.

It remains to show there are no
a\in{\sf Ind}(\mathcal{A}_{c})\setminus{\sf Ind}(\mathcal{A}_{0})

and basic concept
B
with
{\sf sig}(B)\subseteq\Sigma_{\mathsf{C}}
such
that
a\in B^{\mathcal{I}_{\mathcal{T},\mathcal{A}}}
. For a proof by contradiction, assume that there exist an
a\in{\sf Ind}(\mathcal{A}_{c})\setminus{\sf Ind}(\mathcal{A}_{0})

and basic concept
B
with
{\sf sig}(B)\subseteq\Sigma_{\mathsf{C}}
such that
a\in B^{\mathcal{I}_{\mathcal{T},\mathcal{A}}}
.
Let
a
be the first such individual name introduced using Rule (R2) in the construction
of
\mathcal{A}_{c}
. By Point (p2) and the construction of
\mathcal{A}_{c}
there exist
B_{1}
,
r
,
a_{0}
and
j\geq 0
such that

\mathcal{T}\models B_{1}\sqsubseteq\exists r
,
a_{0}\in B_{1}^{\mathcal{I}_{\mathcal{A}_{j}}}
,

a_{0}\not\in(\exists r)^{\mathcal{I}_{\mathcal{A}_{j}}}
,

(a_{0},a)\in r^{\mathcal{I}_{\mathcal{A}_{j+1}}}
,

\mathcal{T}\models\exists r^{-}\sqsubseteq B
.
We show that
B_{1}
,
B_{2}
, and
r
satisfy Conditions 1 to 4 from
Definition
5.2
for
B_{2}:=B
and thus derive a contradiction
to the assumption that
(\mathcal{T},\Sigma_{\mathsf{C}})
is safe. Conditions 1 and
2 are clear. For Condition 3, assume that
B_{1}=\exists r^{\prime}
for
some
r^{\prime}
such that
\mathcal{T}\models r^{\prime}\sqsubseteq r
. Then
(a_{0},e)\in(r^{\prime})^{\mathcal{I}_{\mathcal{A}_{j}}}
for some
e
. But then, since Rule (R1) is exhaustively
applied before Rule (R2) is applied, we have
(a_{0},e)\in r^{\mathcal{I}_{\mathcal{A}_{j}}}
which contradicts
a_{0}\not\in(\exists r)^{\mathcal{I}_{\mathcal{A}_{j}}}
. For Condition 4 assume that
\mathcal{T}\models B_{1}\sqsubseteq\exists r^{\prime}
for some role
r^{\prime}
such that
{\sf sig}(r^{\prime})\subseteq\Sigma_{\mathsf{C}}
and
\mathcal{T}\models r^{\prime}\sqsubseteq r
.
Then
a_{0}\in{\sf Ind}(\mathcal{A})
because otherwise
a_{0}
is an individual name
introduced before
a
such that
a_{0}\in(\exists r^{\prime})^{\mathcal{I}_{\mathcal{T},\mathcal{A}}}
and

{\sf sig}(\exists r^{\prime})\subseteq\Sigma_{\mathsf{C}}
, which contradicts our
assumption about
a
. By Point (p1) and the consistency of
\mathcal{A}

w.r.t.
(\mathcal{T},\Sigma_{\mathsf{C}})
, there is some
b\in\mathsf{Ind}(\mathcal{A})
such
that
(a_{0},b)\in(r^{\prime})^{\mathcal{I}_{\mathcal{A}}}
. But then, again since Rule (R1) is exhaustively
applied before Rule (R2) is applied,
(a_{0},b)\in r^{\mathcal{I}_{\mathcal{A}_{j}}}
which contradicts
a_{0}\not\in(\exists r)^{\mathcal{I}_{\mathcal{A}_{j}}}
.

Observe that we have also proved that if
\mathcal{A}
is consistent w.r.t.
(\mathcal{T},\emptyset)
, then
there do not exist
a\in{\sf Ind}(\mathcal{A}_{c})\setminus{\sf Ind}(\mathcal{A}_{0})

and a basic concept
B
with
{\sf sig}(B)\subseteq\Sigma_{\mathsf{C}}
such that
a\in B^{\mathcal{I}_{\mathcal{T},\mathcal{A}}}
.

As the final step in the proof of Point (2), we show that ABox consistency w.r.t. a safe
\text{DL-Lite}_{\mathcal{R}}
TBox
with closed predicates is FO-rewritable.

Claim 13
.

Let
(\mathcal{T},\Sigma_{\mathsf{C}})
be a safe
\text{DL-Lite}_{\mathcal{R}}
TBox with closed
predicates. Then ABox consistency w.r.t.
(\mathcal{T},\Sigma_{\mathsf{C}})
is FO-rewritable.

Proof 5.6

(Proof of claim.)
.

It follows immediately from the final remark in the proof of Claim 3 above
that an ABox
\mathcal{A}
is consistent w.r.t. a safe

(\mathcal{T},\Sigma_{\mathsf{C}})
if, and only if, (i)
\mathcal{A}
is consistent w.r.t.
(\mathcal{T},\emptyset)
, (ii)
\mathcal{A}\models(\mathcal{T},\emptyset,A(x))(a)
implies
a\in A^{\mathcal{I}_{\mathcal{A}}}
for all concept names
A\in\Sigma_{\mathsf{C}}
, (iii)
\mathcal{A}\models(\mathcal{T},\emptyset,\exists y\,r(x,y))(a)
implies
a\in(\exists r)^{\mathcal{I}_{\mathcal{A}}}
for all roles
r
with
{\sf sig}(r)\subseteq\Sigma_{\mathsf{C}}
,
and (iv)
\mathcal{A}\models(\mathcal{T},\emptyset,r(x,y))(a,b)
implies

(a,b)\in r^{\mathcal{I}_{\mathcal{A}}}
for all role names
r\in\Sigma_{\mathsf{C}}
.

To obtain an FO-rewriting of ABox consistency w.r.t.
(\mathcal{T},\Sigma_{\mathsf{C}})
, let

p_{c}
be an FO-rewriting of
ABox consistency w.r.t.
(\mathcal{T},\emptyset)
, let
q_{A}(x)
be an FO-rewriting of

(\mathcal{T},\emptyset,A(x))
, for
A\in\Sigma_{\mathsf{C}}
,
let
q_{\exists r}(x)
be an FO-rewriting of
(\mathcal{T},\emptyset,\exists y\,r(x,y))
for
r\in\Sigma_{\mathsf{C}}
,
let
q_{\exists r^{-}}(x)
be an FO-rewriting of
(\mathcal{T},\emptyset,\exists y\,r(y,x))
for
r\in\Sigma_{\mathsf{C}}
and let

q_{r}(x,y)=\bigvee_{\mathcal{T}\models s\sqsubseteq r}s(x,y)
, for
r\in\Sigma_{\mathsf{C}}
.
Then
p_{c}\wedge q_{1}\wedge q_{2}\wedge q_{3}\wedge q_{4}

with

\displaystyle q_{1}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\forall x\bigwedge_{A\in\Sigma_{\mathsf{C}}}(q_{A}(x)\rightarrow A(x))

\displaystyle q_{2}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\forall x\bigwedge_{r\in\Sigma_{\mathsf{C}}}(q_{\exists r}(x)\rightarrow\exists y\,r(x,y))

\displaystyle q_{3}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\forall x\bigwedge_{r\in\Sigma_{\mathsf{C}}}(q_{\exists r^{-}}(x)\rightarrow\exists y\,r(y,x))

\displaystyle q_{4}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\forall x\forall y\bigwedge_{r\in\Sigma_{\mathsf{C}}}(q_{r}(x,y)\rightarrow r(x,y))

is an FO-rewriting of ABox consistency w.r.t.
(\mathcal{T},\Sigma_{\mathsf{C}})
.
\dashv

\square

5.3.
Dichotomy for
\mathcal{EL}

We show the announced dichotomy for
\mathcal{EL}
TBoxes with closed
predicates. While we follow the same strategy as in the DL-Lite case,
there are some interesting new aspects. In particular, we identify an
additional reason for
coNP
-hardness that we treat by using a variant
of the Craig interpolation property for
\mathcal{EL}
. We call a concept
E

a
top-level conjunct (tlc) of
an
\mathcal{EL}
concept
C
if
C
is of
the form
D_{1}\sqcap\cdots\sqcap D_{n}
and
E=D_{i}
for some
i
.
We use the following version of safeness.

{defi}

[Safe
\mathcal{EL}
TBox]

An
\mathcal{EL}
TBox with closed predicates
(\mathcal{T},\Sigma_{\mathsf{C}})
is
safe

if there exists no
\mathcal{EL}
concept inclusion
C\sqsubseteq\exists r.D
such
that

(1)

\mathcal{T}\models C\sqsubseteq\exists r.D
;

(2)

there is no tlc
\exists r.C^{\prime}

of
C
with
\mathcal{T}\models C^{\prime}\sqsubseteq D
;

(3)

one of the following is true:

(s1)

r\not\in\Sigma_{\mathsf{C}}
and
{\sf sig}(D)\cap\Sigma_{\mathsf{C}}\not=\emptyset
;

(s2)

r\in\Sigma_{\mathsf{C}}
,
{\sf sig}(D)\not\subseteq\Sigma_{\mathsf{C}}
,
and there is no
\mathcal{EL}
concept
E
with

\mathsf{sig}(E)\subseteq\Sigma_{\mathsf{C}}
,
\mathcal{T}\models C\sqsubseteq\exists r.E
, and
\mathcal{T}\models E\sqsubseteq D
.
\triangle

Condition
3(s1)
captures a reason for
non-convexity that is similar to the
DL-Lite
case. For example,
we can recast Example
5.1
using

\mathcal{T}=\{A\sqsubseteq\exists r.B\}
and

\Sigma_{\mathsf{C}}=\{B\}
. Then the inclusion

A\sqsubseteq\exists r.B
shows that

(\mathcal{T},\Sigma_{\mathsf{C}})
is not safe as
r\not\in\Sigma_{\mathsf{C}}
and

B\in\Sigma_{\mathsf{C}}
. However, in
\mathcal{EL}
there is an additional reason for non-convexity that
is captured by Condition
3(s2)
.
{exa}

Let
\mathcal{T}=\{A\sqsubseteq\exists r.B\}
and
\Sigma_{\mathsf{C}}=\{r\}
. Clearly, by
Condition
3(s2)
,
(\mathcal{T},\Sigma_{\mathsf{C}})
is not safe.
We show that
(\mathcal{T},\Sigma_{\mathsf{C}})
is not convex for dtCQs. Let

\begin{array}[]{rcl}\mathcal{A}&=&\{A(a),r(a,b_{1}),A_{1}(b_{1}),r(a,b_{2}),A_{2}(b_{2})\}\\[2.84526pt]
q_{i}&=&\exists y\,(r(x,y)\wedge A_{i}(y)\wedge B(y))\end{array}

Then
(\mathcal{T},\Sigma_{\mathsf{C}})
is not convex because
\mathcal{A}\models(\mathcal{T},\Sigma_{\mathsf{C}},q_{1}\vee q_{2})(a)
, whereas

\mathcal{A}\not\models(\mathcal{T},\Sigma_{\mathsf{C}},q_{i})(a)
for any
i\in\{1,2\}
.
Observe that one cannot reproduce this example in DL-Lite: for example, for the
TBox
\mathcal{T}^{\prime}=\{A\sqsubseteq\exists r,\exists r^{-}\sqsubseteq B\}
with
\Sigma^{\prime}_{\mathsf{C}}=\{r\}
,
we have
\mathcal{A}\models(\mathcal{T}^{\prime},\Sigma^{\prime}_{\mathsf{C}},B(x))(b_{i})
for
i=1,2
and thus convexity for dtCQs is not violated.

Note that Condition
3(s2)
additionally requires
the non-existence of a certain concept
E
which can be viewed as an
interpolant between
C
and
\exists r.D
that uses only closed
predicates. The following example illustrates why this condition is
needed.

{exa}

Let
\mathcal{T}=\{A\sqsubseteq\exists r.E,E\sqsubseteq B\}
and first
assume that
\Sigma_{\mathsf{C}}=\{r\}
. Then the CI
A\sqsubseteq\exists r.B
satisfies Condition
3(s2)
and thus

(\mathcal{T},\Sigma_{\mathsf{C}})
is not safe.
Now let
\Sigma^{\prime}_{\mathsf{C}}=\{r,E\}
. In this case, the CI

A\sqsubseteq\exists r.B
does not violate safeness because
E
can
be used as a ‘closed interpolant’. Indeed, it is not difficult to
show that
(\mathcal{T},\Sigma^{\prime}_{\mathsf{C}})
is both safe and convex for dtCQs.

We now formulate our dichotomy result for
\mathcal{EL}
.

Theorem 14
.

Let
(\mathcal{T},\Sigma_{\mathsf{C}})
be an
\mathcal{EL}
TBox with closed predicates. Then

(1)

If
(\mathcal{T},\Sigma_{\mathsf{C}})
is not safe, then
(\mathcal{T},\Sigma_{\mathsf{C}})
is not
convex for dtCQs and evaluating dtCQs w.r.t.
(\mathcal{T},\Sigma_{\mathsf{C}})
is

coNP
-hard.

(2)

If
(\mathcal{T},\Sigma_{\mathsf{C}})
is safe, then
(\mathcal{T},\Sigma_{\mathsf{C}})
is convex for tCQs and

(a)

(\mathcal{T},\Sigma_{\mathsf{C}})
and
(\mathcal{T},\emptyset)
are UCQ inseparable on consistent ABoxes.

(b)

UCQ evaluation w.r.t.
(\mathcal{T},\Sigma_{\mathsf{C}})
is in
PTime
.

Towards a proof of Theorem
14
, we start with introducing
canonical models, prove some fundamental lemmas regarding such models,
and establish a variant of the Craig interpolation property for
\mathcal{EL}
that we use to address Condition
3(s2)
of
Definition
5.3
. In fact, we introduce several versions of
canonical models. For the proof of Point (1) of
Theorem
14
, we use
finite
canonical models for
\mathcal{EL}
TBoxes and
\mathcal{EL}
concepts. For Point (2) and for establishing Craig
interpolation, we use (essentially)
tree-shaped
canonical
models of
\mathcal{EL}
TBoxes and possibly infinite ABoxes and,
as a special case, the same kind of canonical models of
\mathcal{EL}
TBoxes and

\mathcal{EL}
concepts. The constructions of all these canonical models does not
involve closed predicates. However, to deal with closed predicates in
the proofs, it turns out that we need a more careful definition of
(tree-shaped) canonical models than usual.

We start with the definition of finite canonical models for
\mathcal{EL}
TBoxes

\mathcal{T}
and
\mathcal{EL}
concepts
C
. Take for every

D\in{\sf sub}(\mathcal{T},C)
an individual name
a_{D}
and define the

canonical model

\mathcal{I}_{\mathcal{T},C}=(\Delta^{\mathcal{I}_{\mathcal{T},C}},\cdot^{\mathcal{I}_{\mathcal{T},C}})
of

\mathcal{T}
and
C
as follows:

•

\Delta^{\mathcal{I}_{\mathcal{T},C}}=\{a_{C}\}\cup\{a_{C^{\prime}}\mid\exists r.C^{\prime}\in\mathsf{sub}(\mathcal{T},C)\}
;

•

a_{D}\in A^{\mathcal{I}_{\mathcal{T},C}}
if
\mathcal{T}\models D\sqsubseteq A
, for all
A\in{\sf N_{C}}
and
a_{D}\in\Delta^{\mathcal{I}_{\mathcal{T},C}}
;

•

(a_{D_{0}},a_{D_{1}})\in r^{{\mathcal{I}}_{\mathcal{T},C}}
if
\mathcal{T}\models D_{0}\sqsubseteq\exists r.D_{1}
and
\exists r.D_{1}\in\mathsf{sub}(\mathcal{T})
or
\exists r.D_{1}
is a tlc of
D_{0}
, for all

a_{D_{0}},a_{D_{1}}\in\Delta^{\mathcal{I}_{\mathcal{T},C}}
and
r\in{\sf N_{R}}
.

Deciding whether
\mathcal{T}\models C\sqsubseteq D
is in
PTime

[
7
]
, and thus
\mathcal{I}_{\mathcal{T},C}

can be constructed in time polynomial in the size of
\mathcal{T}
and
C
.
The following lemma, shown in
[
46
]
as Lemma 12, is the reason
for why
\mathcal{I}_{\mathcal{T},C}
is called a canonical
model.

Lemma 15
.

Let
C
be an
\mathcal{EL}
concept and
\mathcal{T}
an
\mathcal{EL}
TBox. Then

•

\mathcal{I}_{\mathcal{T},C}
is a model of
\mathcal{T}
;

•

for all
D_{0}\in{\sf sub}(\mathcal{T},C)
and
all
\mathcal{EL}
concepts
D_{1}
:
\mathcal{T}\models D_{0}\sqsubseteq D_{1}

iff
a_{D_{0}}\in D_{1}^{\mathcal{I}_{\mathcal{T},C}}
.

The next lemma, shown in
[
46
]
as Lemma 16, is concerned
with the implication of existential restrictions in
\mathcal{EL}
. We will use
it in proofs below. It is proved using Lemma
15

and the construction of canonical models.

Lemma 16
.

Suppose
\mathcal{T}\models C\sqsubseteq\exists r.D
, where
C
,
D

are
\mathcal{EL}
concepts and
\mathcal{T}
is an
\mathcal{EL}
TBox. Then one of the
following holds:

•

there is a tlc
\exists r.C^{\prime}
of
C
such that

\mathcal{T}\models C^{\prime}\sqsubseteq D
;

•

there is a concept
\exists r.C^{\prime}\in\mathsf{sub}(\mathcal{T})
such that

\mathcal{T}\models C\sqsubseteq\exists r.C^{\prime}
and
\mathcal{T}\models C^{\prime}\sqsubseteq D
.

We next construct tree-shaped canonical models. We start with
canonical models
\mathcal{J}_{\mathcal{T},\mathcal{A}}
of an
\mathcal{EL}
TBox
\mathcal{T}

and a (possibly infinite) ABox
\mathcal{A}
.
In the construction of
\mathcal{J}_{\mathcal{T},\mathcal{A}}
, we use
extended
ABoxes
that additionally admit assertions of the form
C(a)
with

C
an arbitrary
\mathcal{EL}
concept. We construct a sequence of extended ABoxes

\mathcal{A}_{0},\mathcal{A}_{1},\dots
, starting with
\mathcal{A}_{0}=\mathcal{A}
. In what
follows, we use additional individual names of the form

a\cdot r_{1}\cdot C_{1}\cdots r_{k}\cdot C_{k}
with

a\in\mathsf{Ind}(\mathcal{A}_{0})
,
r_{1},\dots,r_{k}
role names that occur in

\mathcal{T}
, and
C_{1},\dots,C_{k}\in\mathsf{sub}(\mathcal{T})
. We set

{\sf tail}(a\cdot r_{1}\cdot C_{1}\cdots r_{k}\cdot C_{k})=C_{k}
. Each
extended ABox
\mathcal{A}_{i+1}
is obtained from
\mathcal{A}_{i}
by applying the
following rules (the interpretation
\mathcal{I}_{\mathcal{A}_{i}}
corresponding to
the extended ABox
\mathcal{A}_{i}
ignores assertions
C(a)
with
C
not a concept name):

(R1)

if
C\sqcap D(a)\in\mathcal{A}_{i}
, then add
C(a)
and

D(a)
to
\mathcal{A}_{i}
;

(R2)

if
a\in C^{\mathcal{I}_{\mathcal{A}_{i}}}

and
C\sqsubseteq D\in\mathcal{T}
, then add
D(a)
to
\mathcal{A}_{i}
;

(R3)

if
\exists r.C(a)\in\mathcal{A}_{i}
and there exist
b\in\mathsf{Ind}(\mathcal{A}_{i})
with
r(a,b)\in\mathcal{A}_{i}
and
\mathcal{A}_{i}\models(\mathcal{T},\emptyset,q_{C})(b)
, then add
C(b)
to
\mathcal{A}_{i}
; otherwise add
r(a,a\cdot r\cdot C)
and
C(a\cdot r\cdot C)
to
\mathcal{A}_{i}
. (Recall that
q_{C}
denotes the directed tree CQ corresponding to the concept
C
.)

Let
\mathcal{A}_{c}=\bigcup_{i\geq 0}\mathcal{A}_{i}
. Note that
\mathcal{A}_{c}
may be
infinite even if
\mathcal{A}
is finite. Also note that rule (R3) carefully
avoids to introduce fresh successors as witnesses for existential
restrictions when this is not strictly necessary. This will be useful
when closing predicates which might preclude the introduction of
fresh successors.
Let
\mathcal{J}_{\mathcal{T},\mathcal{A}}

be the interpretation that corresponds to
\mathcal{A}_{c}
.
Points 1 and 2 of the following lemma show that
\mathcal{J}_{\mathcal{T},\mathcal{A}}

is canonical, essentially in the sense of
Lemma
15
, and Points 3 and 4 show that, in
addition, it is
universal
for UCQs: answers given by

\mathcal{J}_{\mathcal{T},\mathcal{A}}
coincide with the certain answers.

Lemma 17
.

Let
\mathcal{T}
be an
\mathcal{EL}
TBox and
\mathcal{A}
a possibly infinite ABox. Then

(1)

\mathcal{J}_{\mathcal{T},\mathcal{A}}
is a model of
\mathcal{T}
and
\mathcal{A}
;

(2)

for all
p\in\Delta^{\mathcal{J}_{\mathcal{T},\mathcal{A}}}\setminus{\sf Ind}(\mathcal{A})
and all
\mathcal{EL}
concepts
D
:
p\in D^{\mathcal{J}_{\mathcal{T},\mathcal{A}}}
iff
\mathcal{T}\models{\sf tail}(p)\sqsubseteq D
;

(3)

for every model
\mathcal{I}
of
\mathcal{T}
and
\mathcal{A}
, there is a
homomorphism
h
from
\mathcal{J}_{\mathcal{T},\mathcal{A}}
to
\mathcal{I}
that preserves

\mathsf{Ind}(\mathcal{A})
;

(4)

for all UCQs
q(\vec{x})
and tuples
\vec{a}
in
\mathsf{Ind}(\mathcal{A})
:

\mathcal{A}\models(\mathcal{T},\emptyset,q)(\vec{a})
iff
\mathcal{J}_{\mathcal{T},\mathcal{A}}\models q(\vec{a})
.

We now construct tree-shaped canonical models
\mathcal{J}_{\mathcal{T},C}

of an
\mathcal{EL}
TBox
\mathcal{T}
and an
\mathcal{EL}

concept

C
.
A
path
in
C

is a finite sequence
C_{0}\cdot r_{1}\cdot C_{1}\cdots r_{n}\cdot C_{n}
, where
C_{0}=C
,
n\geq 0
, and
\exists r_{i+1}.C_{i+1}
is a tlc of
C_{i}
, for
0\leq i<n
. We use
{\sf paths}(C)
to denote the set of paths in

C
.
If
p\in{\sf paths}(C)
, then
{\sf tail}(p)
denotes the last element of

p
.
The
ABox

\mathcal{A}_{C}
associated with
C
is defined by setting

\displaystyle\mathcal{A}_{C}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\{r(p,q)\mid p,q\in{\sf paths}(C);q=p\cdot r\cdot C^{\prime}\}

\displaystyle\{A(p)\mid A\mbox{ a tlc of }{\sf tail}(p),p\in{\sf paths}(C)\}.

Then
\mathcal{J}_{\mathcal{T},C}:=\mathcal{J}_{\mathcal{T},\mathcal{A}_{C}}
is the
tree-shaped canonical model
of
\mathcal{T}
and
C
.
The following is an easy consequence of Lemma
17
.

Lemma 18
.

Let
\mathcal{T}
be an
\mathcal{EL}
TBox and
C
an
\mathcal{EL}
concept. Then

•

\mathcal{J}_{\mathcal{T},C}
is a model of
\mathcal{T}
;

•

for all
p\in\Delta^{\mathcal{J}_{\mathcal{T},C}}
and all
\mathcal{EL}
concepts

D
:
p\in D^{\mathcal{J}_{\mathcal{T},C}}
iff
\mathcal{T}\models{\sf tail}(p)\sqsubseteq D
.

We next give a lemma that connects an answers
a

to a dtCQs
q_{C}

on an ABox
\mathcal{A}
under a TBox
\mathcal{T}
with the entailment by
\mathcal{T}
of
concept inclusions of the form
C_{a}^{m}\sqsubseteq C
where
C_{a}^{m}
is obtained by unfolding
\mathcal{A}
at

a
up to depth
m
. More precisely, for every
m\geq 0
define

C_{a}^{0}=(\bigsqcap_{A(a)\in\mathcal{A}}A),\quad C_{a}^{m+1}=(\bigsqcap_{A(a)\in\mathcal{A}}A)\sqcap(\bigsqcap_{r(a,b)\in\mathcal{A}}\exists r.C_{b}^{m}).

The following is shown in
[
46
]
as Lemma 22.

Lemma 19
.

For all
\mathcal{EL}
TBoxes
\mathcal{T}
,
\mathcal{EL}
concepts
C
, ABoxes
\mathcal{A}
, and
a\in{\sf Ind}(\mathcal{A})
:

\mathcal{A}\models(\mathcal{T},\emptyset,q_{C})(a)\quad\text{ iff }\quad\exists m\geq 0:\quad\mathcal{T}\models C_{a}^{m}\sqsubseteq C

We now establish a variant of the Craig interpolation property that is
suitable for addressing Condition
3(s2)
of
Definition
5.3
. It has been studied before for
\mathcal{ALC}

and several of its extensions in the context of query rewriting for
DBoxes and of Beth definability
[
36
,
57
]
.
Note that it is different from the interpolation property investigated
in
[
46
]
for
\mathcal{EL}
, which requires the interpolant to be a TBox
instead of a concept. For brevity, we set
{\sf sig}(\mathcal{T},C)={\sf sig}(\mathcal{T})\cup{\sf sig}(C)
for any TBox
\mathcal{T}
and concept
C
.

Lemma 20

(
\mathcal{EL}
Interpolation)
.

Let
\mathcal{T}_{1},\mathcal{T}_{2}
be
\mathcal{EL}
TBoxes and let

D_{1},D_{2}
be
\mathcal{EL}
concepts with
\mathcal{T}_{1}\cup\mathcal{T}_{2}\models D_{1}\sqsubseteq D_{2}
and
{\sf sig}(\mathcal{T}_{1},D_{1})\cap{\sf sig}(\mathcal{T}_{2},D_{2})=\Sigma
. Then there exists an
\mathcal{EL}
concept
F
such that
\mathsf{sig}(F)\subseteq\Sigma
,

\mathcal{T}_{1}\cup\mathcal{T}_{2}\models D_{1}\sqsubseteq F
, and

\mathcal{T}_{1}\cup\mathcal{T}_{2}\models F\sqsubseteq D_{2}
.

Proof 5.7
.

Let
\mathcal{T}_{1}\cup\mathcal{T}_{2}\models D_{1}\sqsubseteq D_{2}
with

{\sf sig}(\mathcal{T}_{1},D_{1})\cap{\sf sig}(\mathcal{T}_{2},D_{2})=\Sigma
.
Assume that the required
\mathcal{EL}
concept
F
does not exist. Consider
the tree-shaped canonical model
\mathcal{J}_{\mathcal{T}_{1}\cup\mathcal{T}_{2},D_{1}}
.
Denote by
\mathcal{A}_{\Sigma}
the ABox corresponding to the

\Sigma
-reduct of
\mathcal{J}_{\mathcal{T}_{1}\cup\mathcal{T}_{2},D_{1}}
, thus

\mathcal{A}_{\Sigma}=\bigcup_{A\in\Sigma}\{A(a)\mid a\in A^{\mathcal{J}_{\mathcal{T}_{1}\cup\mathcal{T}_{2},D_{1}}}\}\cup\bigcup_{r\in\Sigma}\{r(a,b)\mid r(a,b)\in r^{\mathcal{J}_{\mathcal{T}_{1}\cup\mathcal{T}_{2},D_{1}}}\}

We may assume w.l.o.g. that
{\sf Ind}(\mathcal{A}_{\Sigma})=\Delta^{\mathcal{J}_{\mathcal{T}_{1}\cup\mathcal{T}_{2},D_{1}}}
. Recall that
the individual names in
\mathcal{A}_{\Sigma}
are paths. For the sake of readability, we denote them by
a_{p}

rather than
p
. Also recall that
q_{D}
denotes the dtCQ corresponding to the
\mathcal{EL}
concept
D
.

Claim
.

\mathcal{A}_{\Sigma}\not\models(\mathcal{T}_{1}\cup\mathcal{T}_{2},\emptyset,q_{D_{2}})(a_{D_{1}})
.

Proof 5.8

(Proof of claim.)
.

Assume for a proof by contradiction that
\mathcal{A}_{\Sigma}\models(\mathcal{T}_{1}\cup\mathcal{T}_{2},\emptyset,q_{D_{2}})(a_{D_{1}})
.
By Lemma
19
, there is an
\mathcal{EL}
concept
F
such that

\mathsf{sig}(F)\subseteq\Sigma
,
\mathcal{T}_{1}\cup\mathcal{T}_{2}\models F\sqsubseteq D_{2}
, and
a_{D_{1}}\in F^{\mathcal{J}_{\mathcal{T}_{1}\cup\mathcal{T}_{2},D_{1}}}
. Then, using Lemma
18
, we
obtain
\mathcal{T}_{1}\cup\mathcal{T}_{2}\models D_{1}\sqsubseteq F
. This contradicts our assumption that no such concept
F
exists.

Obviously,
\mathcal{J}_{\mathcal{T}_{1}\cup\mathcal{T}_{2},D_{1}}
is a model of

\mathcal{T}_{1}\cup\mathcal{T}_{2}
and
\mathcal{A}_{\Sigma}
. Then, by
Lemma
17
, there is a homomorphism
h
from

\mathcal{J}_{\mathcal{T}_{1}\cup\mathcal{T}_{2},\mathcal{A}_{\Sigma}}
to

\mathcal{J}_{\mathcal{T}_{1}\cup\mathcal{T}_{2},D_{1}}
with
h(a)=a
for all

a\in\mathsf{Ind}(\mathcal{A}_{\Sigma})
. Conversely,
h^{\prime}=\{a\mapsto a\mid a\in\Delta^{\mathcal{J}_{\mathcal{T}_{1}\cup\mathcal{T}_{2},D_{1}}}\}
is a
homomorphism from the
\Sigma
-reduct of
\mathcal{J}_{\mathcal{T}_{1}\cup\mathcal{T}_{2},D_{1}}

to
\mathcal{J}_{\mathcal{T}_{1}\cup\mathcal{T}_{2},\mathcal{A}_{\Sigma}}
. Define the interpretation
\mathcal{I}
as follows:

\displaystyle\Delta^{\mathcal{I}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\Delta^{\mathcal{J}_{\mathcal{T}_{1}\cup\mathcal{T}_{2},\mathcal{A}_{\Sigma}}}

\displaystyle P^{\mathcal{I}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle P^{\mathcal{J}_{\mathcal{T}_{1}\cup\mathcal{T}_{2},\mathcal{A}_{\Sigma}}}\cup P^{\mathcal{J}_{\mathcal{T}_{1}\cup\mathcal{T}_{2},D_{1}}},\text{ for all $P\in\mathsf{sig}(\mathcal{T}_{1},D_{1})\setminus\Sigma$}

\displaystyle P^{\mathcal{I}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle P^{\mathcal{J}_{\mathcal{T}_{1}\cup\mathcal{T}_{2},\mathcal{A}_{\Sigma}}},\text{ for all $P\not\in\mathsf{sig}(\mathcal{T}_{1},D_{1})\setminus\Sigma$}

Observe that the mapping
h
defined above is a homomorphism from
\mathcal{I}
to

\mathcal{J}_{\mathcal{T}_{1}\cup\mathcal{T}_{2},D_{1}}
with
h(a)=a
for all

a\in\mathsf{Ind}(\mathcal{A}_{\Sigma})
. Conversely, the mapping
h^{\prime}
defined above is a homomorphism from the
\mathsf{sig}(\mathcal{T}_{1},D_{1})
-reduct of

\mathcal{J}_{\mathcal{T}_{1}\cup\mathcal{T}_{2},D_{1}}
to
\mathcal{I}
. Now it is readily checked that
\mathcal{EL}
concepts
C
are
preserved under homomorphisms (if
d\in C^{\mathcal{I}_{1}}
,
then
h(d)\in C^{\mathcal{I}_{2}}
if
h
is a homomorphism from
\mathcal{I}_{1}
to
\mathcal{I}_{2}
).
Thus,
\mathcal{I}
is a model of
\mathcal{T}_{1}
since
\mathcal{J}_{\mathcal{T}_{1}\cup\mathcal{T}_{2},D_{1}}
is a model of
\mathcal{T}_{1}
and

a_{D_{1}}\in D_{1}^{\mathcal{I}}
since
a_{D_{1}}\in D_{1}^{\mathcal{J}_{\mathcal{T}_{1}\cup\mathcal{T}_{2},D_{1}}}
. Moreover, by construction,
\mathcal{I}
is a model of
\mathcal{T}_{2}
and

a_{D_{1}}\not\in D_{2}^{\mathcal{I}}
, by the claim proved above.
We have shown that
\mathcal{T}_{1}\cup\mathcal{T}_{2}\not\models D_{1}\sqsubseteq D_{2}
and thus derived a contradiction.

We are now in the position to prove Theorem
14
. We first prove Point (1).
The proof requires two separate constructions that both show non-convexity for dtCQs and address
Cases
3(s1)
and
3(s2)
from
Definition
5.3
. It then follows from Lemma
8
that
dtCQ evaluation w.r.t. (
\mathcal{T},\Sigma_{\mathsf{C}})
is
coNP
-hard.

We begin by considering Case
3(s1)
.

Lemma 21
.

Let
(\mathcal{T},\Sigma_{\mathsf{C}})
be an
\mathcal{EL}
TBox with closed
predicates such that safeness is violated by an inclusion
C\sqsubseteq\exists r.D
because Condition
3(s1)
from Definition
5.3

holds. Then
(\mathcal{T},\Sigma_{\mathsf{C}})
is not
convex for dtCQs.

Proof 5.9
.

Assume
C\sqsubseteq\exists r.D
satisfies
\mathcal{T}\models C\sqsubseteq\exists r.D
,
there is no tlc
\exists r.C^{\prime}
of
C
with
\mathcal{T}\models C^{\prime}\sqsubseteq D
,
r\not\in\Sigma_{\mathsf{C}}
,
and
{\sf sig}(D)\cap\Sigma_{\mathsf{C}}\not=\emptyset
.
Consider the finite canonical model

\mathcal{I}_{\mathcal{T},C}
of
\mathcal{T}
and
C
. Assume w.l.o.g. that
C
does not
occur in
\mathcal{T}
(if it does, replace
C
by
A\sqcap C
for a fresh
concept name
A
). Note that it follows that there is no

a\in\Delta^{\mathcal{I}_{\mathcal{T},C}}
with
(a,a_{C})\in s^{\mathcal{I}_{\mathcal{T},C}}
for any role name
s
.

Let
\mathcal{I}_{r}
be the interpretation obtained from
\mathcal{I}_{\mathcal{T},C}
by removing all pairs
(a_{C},a_{E})

from
r^{\mathcal{I}_{\mathcal{T},C}}
such that
\exists r.E
is not a tlc of
C
. Let
\mathcal{A}_{r}
be the ABox corresponding
to
\mathcal{I}_{r}
and let
\mathcal{A}
be the disjoint union of
two copies of
\mathcal{A}_{r}
. We denote the individual names of the first copy by
(a,1)
,
a\in\Delta^{\mathcal{I}_{\mathcal{T},C}}
, and the
individual names of the second copy by
(a,2)
,
a\in\Delta^{\mathcal{I}_{\mathcal{T},C}}
. Let
A_{1}
and
A_{2}
be fresh concept
names and set

\mathcal{A}^{\prime}=\mathcal{A}\cup\{A_{1}(a,1)\mid a\in\Delta^{\mathcal{I}_{\mathcal{T},C}}\}\cup\{A_{2}(a,2)\mid a\in\Delta^{\mathcal{I}_{\mathcal{T},C}}\}

Some predicate
P\in\Sigma_{\mathsf{C}}
occurs in
D
. If a concept name
E\in\Sigma_{\mathsf{C}}
occurs in
D
, then fix one such

E
and denote, for
i\in\{1,2\}
, by
D_{i}
the resulting concept after one occurrence
of
E
is replaced by
A_{i}\sqcap E
. For example, if
D=A\sqcap\exists s_{1}.E\sqcap\exists s_{2}.E
,
E\in\Sigma_{\mathsf{C}}

and
A\not\in\Sigma_{\mathsf{C}}
, then either
D_{i}=A\sqcap\exists s_{1}.(A_{i}\sqcap E)\sqcap\exists s_{2}.E
or

D_{i}=A\sqcap\exists s_{1}.E\sqcap\exists s_{2}.(A_{i}\sqcap E)
. Similarly, if no concept name
from
\Sigma_{\mathsf{C}}
occurs in
D
, then let
s\in\Sigma_{\mathsf{C}}
be a role name such that a
concept of the form
\exists s.G
occurs in
D
. Denote by
D_{i}

the resulting concept after one occurrence of
\exists s.G
is
replaced by
A_{i}\sqcap\exists s.G
.

We now use
\mathcal{A}^{\prime}
and the dtCQs
q_{\exists r.D_{i}}
to prove that
(\mathcal{T},\Sigma_{\mathsf{C}})
is not convex for dtCQs.
Using the condition
\mathcal{T}\models C\sqsubseteq\exists r.D
and the construction of
D_{1}
and
D_{2}
,
it is straightforward to show that
\mathcal{A}^{\prime}\models(\mathcal{T},\Sigma_{\mathsf{C}},q_{\exists r.D_{1}}\vee q_{\exists r.D_{2}})(a_{C},1)
.
We show that
\mathcal{A}^{\prime}\not\models(\mathcal{T},\Sigma_{\mathsf{C}},q_{\exists r.D_{i}})(a_{C},1)
for
i=1,2
.
Let
i=1
(the case
i=2
is similar and omitted). We construct a model
\mathcal{J}
of

\mathcal{T}
and
\mathcal{A}^{\prime}
that respects
\Sigma_{\mathsf{C}}
with
(a_{C},1)\not\in(\exists r.D_{1})^{\mathcal{J}}
.

\mathcal{J}
is defined as the interpretation corresponding to the ABox
\mathcal{A}^{\prime}
extended by

\{r((a_{C},1),(e_{E},2)),r((a_{C},2),(a_{E},1))\mid(a_{C},a_{E})\in r^{\mathcal{I}_{\mathcal{T},C}}\setminus r^{\mathcal{I}_{r}}\}

Using the fact that
\mathcal{I}_{\mathcal{T},C}
is a model of
\mathcal{T}
it is readily checked that

\mathcal{J}
is a model of
\mathcal{T}
and
\mathcal{A}^{\prime}
that respects
\Sigma_{\mathsf{C}}
. Moreover,

(a_{C},1)\not\in(\exists r.D_{1})^{\mathcal{J}}
. To prove this assume

(a_{C},1)\in(\exists r.D_{1})^{\mathcal{J}}
. Then one of the following
two conditions holds:

•

there exists a tlc
\exists r.E
of
C
such that

(a_{E},1)\in D_{1}^{\mathcal{J}}
;

•

there exists
a_{E}
with
(a_{C},a_{E})\in r^{\mathcal{I}_{\mathcal{T},C}}

such that
(a_{E},2)\in D_{1}^{\mathcal{J}}
.

If the first condition holds, then
a_{E}\in D^{\mathcal{I}_{\mathcal{T},C}}
. Then, by
Lemma
15
,
\mathcal{T}\models E\sqsubseteq D
for
a tlc
\exists r.E
of
C
which contradicts Point (2) of the definition of safeness.
The second condition does not
hold since
(a_{E},2)\in G^{\mathcal{J}}
iff
(a_{E},2)\in G^{\mathcal{J}|_{\{(a,2)\mid a\in\Delta^{\mathcal{I}}\}}}
,
for every
\mathcal{EL}
concept
G
, and
A_{1}^{\mathcal{J}}\cap\{(a,2)\mid a\in\Delta^{\mathcal{I}}\}=\emptyset
,
but
D_{1}
contains
A_{1}
.

We now consider Case
3(s2)
from Definition
5.3
.

Lemma 22
.

Let
(\mathcal{T},\Sigma_{\mathsf{C}})
be an
\mathcal{EL}
TBox with closed
predicates such that safeness is violated by an inclusion
C\sqsubseteq\exists r.D
because Condition
3(s2)
from Definition
5.3

holds. Then
(\mathcal{T},\Sigma_{\mathsf{C}})
is not
convex for dtCQs.

Proof 5.10
.

Assume
C\sqsubseteq\exists r.D
satisfies
\mathcal{T}\models C\sqsubseteq\exists r.D
,
there is no tlc
\exists r.C^{\prime}
of
C
with
\mathcal{T}\models C^{\prime}\sqsubseteq D
, and
Condition
3(s2)
holds.
Let

K=\{G\mid\exists r.G\in{\sf sub}(\mathcal{T}),\mathcal{T}\models C\sqsubseteq\exists r.G\}

Observe that since there is no tlc
\exists r.C^{\prime}
of
C
with
\mathcal{T}\models C^{\prime}\sqsubseteq D
, by Lemma
16
, there exists

G\in K
with
\mathcal{T}\models G\sqsubseteq D
. We now apply the interpolation lemma.
Obtain
\mathcal{T}^{i}
from
\mathcal{T}
by replacing every predicate
P\not\in\Sigma_{\mathsf{C}}
by a
fresh predicate
P_{i}
of the same arity,
i\in\{1,2\}
. Similarly, for any
\mathcal{EL}
concept

F
we denote by
F^{i}
the resulting concept when every predicate
P\not\in\Sigma_{\mathsf{C}}
is
replaced by a fresh predicate
P_{i}
of the same arity,
i\in\{1,2\}
. We show the following
using the interpolation lemma.

Claim 23
.

For all
G\in K
:
\mathcal{T}^{1}\cup\mathcal{T}^{2}\not\models G^{1}\sqsubseteq D^{2}
.

Proof 5.11

(Proof of claim.)
.

The proof is indirect. Assume there exists
G\in K
such that
\mathcal{T}^{1}\cup\mathcal{T}^{2}\models G^{1}\sqsubseteq D^{2}
. By Lemma
20
, there exists an
\mathcal{EL}
concept

F
with
{\sf sig}(F)\subseteq\Sigma_{\mathsf{C}}

such that
\mathcal{T}^{1}\cup\mathcal{T}^{2}\models G^{1}\sqsubseteq F
and

\mathcal{T}^{1}\cup\mathcal{T}^{2}\models F\sqsubseteq D^{2}
. Then
\mathcal{T}\models G\sqsubseteq F
and
\mathcal{T}\models F\sqsubseteq D
. But then we obtain
from
\mathcal{T}\models C\sqsubseteq\exists r.G
that
\mathcal{T}\models C\sqsubseteq\exists r.F
which contradicts Condition
3(s2)
.

By Claim 1 we can take the finite canonical models

\mathcal{J}_{G}:=\mathcal{I}_{\mathcal{T}^{1}\cup\mathcal{T}^{2},G^{1}}
,
G\in K
, and
obtain for
a_{G}:=a_{G^{0}}
that
a_{G}\not\in(D^{2})^{\mathcal{J}_{G}}
.
Let
\mathcal{A}_{G,\Sigma_{\mathsf{C}}}
be the ABox corresponding to the
\Sigma_{\mathsf{C}}
-reduct
of
\mathcal{J}_{G}
. We may assume that the sets of individual names
{\sf Ind}(\mathcal{A}_{G,\Sigma_{\mathsf{C}}})
are
mutually disjoint, for
G\in K
, and that
a_{G}\in{\sf Ind}(\mathcal{A}_{G,\Sigma_{\mathsf{C}}})
, for all
G\in K
.

Claim 24
.

For every
G\in K
, there exist

•

a model
\mathcal{I}_{G}^{1}
of
\mathcal{T}
and
\mathcal{A}_{G,\Sigma_{\mathsf{C}}}
that respects the closed predicates
\Sigma_{\mathsf{C}}

such that
\Delta^{\mathcal{I}_{G}^{1}}={\sf Ind}(\mathcal{A}_{G,\Sigma_{\mathsf{C}}})
,
a_{G}\in G^{\mathcal{I}_{G}^{1}}
,
and
a_{G}\in H^{\mathcal{I}_{G}^{1}}
only if
\mathcal{T}\models G\sqsubseteq H
, for all

\mathcal{EL}
concepts
H
;

•

a model
\mathcal{I}_{G}^{2}
of
\mathcal{T}
and
\mathcal{A}_{G,\Sigma_{\mathsf{C}}}
that respects the closed predicates
\Sigma_{\mathsf{C}}

such that
\Delta^{\mathcal{I}_{G}^{2}}={\sf Ind}(\mathcal{A}_{G,\Sigma_{\mathsf{C}}})
,
a_{G}\not\in D^{\mathcal{I}_{G}^{2}}
, and
a_{G}\in H^{\mathcal{I}_{G}^{2}}

only if
\mathcal{T}\models G\sqsubseteq H
, for all
\mathcal{EL}
concepts
H
.

Proof 5.12

(Proof of claim.)
.

The interpretation
\mathcal{I}_{G}^{1}
is obtained from
\mathcal{J}_{G}
by setting
P^{\mathcal{I}_{G}^{1}}:=(P^{1})^{\mathcal{J}_{G}}
for all predicates
P\in{\sf sig}(\mathcal{T},C,D)\setminus\Sigma_{\mathsf{C}}

and
P^{\mathcal{I}_{G}^{1}}:=\emptyset
for all predicates
P
not in
{\sf sig}(\mathcal{T},C,D)\cup\Sigma_{\mathsf{C}}
. The properties stated follow from the properties of the finite canonical
model
\mathcal{I}_{\mathcal{T}^{1}\cup\mathcal{T}^{2},G^{1}}
. In particular,
a_{G}\in H^{\mathcal{I}_{G}^{1}}
only if
\mathcal{T}\models G\sqsubseteq H
follows from Lemma
16
, Point 2.
The interpretation
\mathcal{I}_{G}^{2}
is obtained from
\mathcal{J}_{G}
by setting
P^{\mathcal{I}_{G}^{2}}:=(P^{2})^{\mathcal{J}_{G}}
for all predicates
P\in{\sf sig}(\mathcal{T},C,D)\setminus\Sigma_{\mathsf{C}}

and
P^{\mathcal{I}_{G}^{2}}:=\emptyset
for all predicates
P
not in
{\sf sig}(\mathcal{T},C,D)\cup\Sigma_{\mathsf{C}}
. The properties stated follow again
from the properties of the finite canonical model
\mathcal{I}_{\mathcal{T}^{1}\cup\mathcal{T}^{2},G^{1}}
.

Introduce two copies
\mathcal{A}_{G,\Sigma_{\mathsf{C}}}^{1}
and
\mathcal{A}_{G,\Sigma_{\mathsf{C}}}^{2}

of
\mathcal{A}_{G,\Sigma_{\mathsf{C}}}
, for
G\in K
. We denote the individual names of the
first copy by
(a,1)
, for
a\in{\sf Ind}(\mathcal{A}_{G,\Sigma_{\mathsf{C}}})
, and the
individual names of the second copy by
(a,2)
, for
a\in{\sf Ind}(\mathcal{A}_{G,\Sigma_{\mathsf{C}}})
.
Let
\mathcal{A}_{r}
be the ABox defined in the beginning of the proof of Lemma
21
.
Define the ABox
\mathcal{A}
by taking two fresh concept names
A_{1}
and
A_{2}
and adding
to

\mathcal{A}_{r}\cup\bigcup_{G\in K}\mathcal{A}_{G,\Sigma_{\mathsf{C}}}^{1}\cup\mathcal{A}_{G,\Sigma_{\mathsf{C}}}^{2}

the assertions

•

r(a_{C},(a_{G},1)),r(a_{C},(a_{G},2))
, for every
G\in K
;

•

A_{1}(a_{G},1)
, for every
G\in K
;

•

A_{1}(a_{E})
, for every tlc
\exists r.E
of
C
;

•

A_{2}(a_{G},2)
, for every
G\in K
.

We use
\mathcal{A}
and the dtCQs
q_{\exists r.(A_{i}\sqcap D)}
to show that
(\mathcal{T},\Sigma_{\mathsf{C}})
is not convex for dtCQs.
The proof that
\mathcal{A}\models(\mathcal{T},\Sigma_{\mathsf{C}},q_{\exists r.(A_{1}\sqcap D)}\vee q_{\exists r.(A_{2}\sqcap D)})(a_{C})
is straightforward
using the condition that
\mathcal{T}\models C\sqsubseteq\exists r.D
and the construction of
\mathcal{A}
(
r\in\Sigma_{\mathsf{C}}
and all
r
-successors
of
a_{C}
in
\mathcal{A}
are either in
A_{1}
or in
A_{2}
).
It remains to show
\mathcal{A}\not\models(\mathcal{T},\Sigma_{\mathsf{C}},q_{\exists r.(A_{i}\sqcap D)})(a_{C})
, for
i=1,2
.
For
i=2
, construct a witness interpretation
\mathcal{J}
showing this
by expanding all
\mathcal{A}_{G,\Sigma_{\mathsf{C}}}^{2}
,
G\in K
, to
(isomorphic copies of)
\mathcal{I}_{G}^{2}
, all
\mathcal{A}_{G,\Sigma_{\mathsf{C}}}^{1}
,
G\in K
, to (isomorphic copies of)
\mathcal{I}_{G}^{1}
, and

\mathcal{A}_{r}
to
\mathcal{I}_{r}
. Using the properties of
\mathcal{I}_{G}^{1}
and

\mathcal{I}_{G}^{2}
established in the claim above, it is readily checked that
\mathcal{J}
is a
model of
\mathcal{T}
and
\mathcal{A}
that respects
\Sigma_{\mathsf{C}}
. Moreover,
a_{C}\not\in(\exists r.(A_{2}\sqcap D))^{\mathcal{J}}

since
(a_{G},2)\not\in D^{\mathcal{J}}
for any
G\in K
(by Claim 2).

For
i=1
, construct a witness interpretation
\mathcal{J}
showing this by
expanding all
\mathcal{A}_{G,\Sigma_{\mathsf{C}}}^{2}
,
G\in K
, to (isomorphic copies of)
\mathcal{I}_{G}^{1}
,
all
\mathcal{A}_{G,\Sigma_{\mathsf{C}}}^{1}
,
G\in K
, to (isomorphic copies of)
\mathcal{I}_{G}^{2}
, and
\mathcal{A}_{r}
to
\mathcal{I}_{r}
.
Using again the properties of
\mathcal{I}_{G}^{1}
and
\mathcal{I}_{G}^{2}
established above, it can
be checked that
\mathcal{J}
is a model of
\mathcal{T}
and
\mathcal{A}
that respects
\Sigma_{\mathsf{C}}
.

a_{C}\not\in(\exists r.(A_{1}\sqcap D))^{\mathcal{J}}
since
a_{E}\not\in D^{\mathcal{J}}
for any tlc
\exists r.E
of

C
and since
(a_{G},1)\not\in D^{\mathcal{J}}
for any
G\in K
(by Claim 2).
This finishes the proof.

This finishes the proof of Point (1) of Theorem
14
. We now prove Part (a) of Point (2).
The proof strategy is exactly the same as in the proof for
\text{DL-Lite}_{\mathcal{R}}
.

Lemma 25
.

Let
(\mathcal{T},\Sigma_{\mathsf{C}})
be a safe
\mathcal{EL}
TBox with closed
predicates. Then for every UCQ
q
, we have

\mathcal{A}\models(\mathcal{T},\Sigma_{\mathsf{C}},q)(\vec{a})\quad\text{ iff
}\quad\mathcal{A}\models(\mathcal{T},\emptyset,q)(\vec{a})

for all ABoxes
\mathcal{A}
that are consistent w.r.t.
(\mathcal{T},\Sigma_{\mathsf{C}})
and all
\vec{a}\in\mathsf{Ind}(\mathcal{A})
.

Proof 5.13
.

Let
(\mathcal{T},\Sigma_{\mathsf{C}})
be safe and assume that
\mathcal{A}
is consistent
w.r.t.
(\mathcal{T},\Sigma_{\mathsf{C}})
.
We consider the tree-shaped canonical model
\mathcal{J}_{\mathcal{T},\mathcal{A}}
introduced above. It suffices to show that

\mathcal{J}_{\mathcal{T},\mathcal{A}}
respects
\Sigma_{\mathsf{C}}
. To this end it suffices to prove for all
A,r\in\Sigma_{\mathsf{C}}
:

(1)

for all
a\in{\sf Ind}(\mathcal{A})
, if
a\in A^{\mathcal{J}_{\mathcal{T},\mathcal{A}}}
, then
A(a)\in\mathcal{A}
;

(2)

for all
a,b\in{\sf Ind}(\mathcal{A})
, if
r(a,b)\in r^{\mathcal{J}_{\mathcal{T},\mathcal{A}}}
, then
r(a,b)\in\mathcal{A}
;

(3)

for all
a\in{\sf Ind}(\mathcal{A})
and
C\in{\sf sub}(\mathcal{T})
,
a\cdot r\cdot C\not\in\Delta^{\mathcal{J}_{\mathcal{T},\mathcal{A}}}
;

(4)

for all
d\in\Delta^{\mathcal{J}_{\mathcal{T},\mathcal{A}}}\setminus\mathsf{Ind}(\mathcal{A})
,
there is no
\mathcal{EL}
concept
D
with
d\in D^{\mathcal{J}_{\mathcal{T},\mathcal{A}}}

and
\mathsf{sig}(D)\cap\Sigma_{\mathsf{C}}\neq\emptyset
.

For Item (1), assume
a\in A^{\mathcal{J}_{\mathcal{T},\mathcal{A}}}
. By Lemma
17
,
\mathcal{A}\models(\mathcal{T},\emptyset,A(x))(a)
,
and so we obtain
\mathcal{A}\models(\mathcal{T},\Sigma_{\mathsf{C}},A(x))(a)
. By consistency of
\mathcal{A}
w.r.t.
(\mathcal{T},\Sigma_{\mathsf{C}})
,
we then have
A(a)\in\mathcal{A}
. Item (2) follows directly from the construction of
\mathcal{J}_{\mathcal{T},\mathcal{A}}
.
For Item (3), assume for a proof by contradiction that there are
a\in{\sf Ind}(\mathcal{A})
,
r\in\Sigma_{\mathsf{C}}
, and a concept
C

such that
a\cdot r\cdot C\in\Delta^{\mathcal{J}_{\mathcal{T},\mathcal{A}}}
. By Lemma
17
, we have
\mathcal{A}\models(\mathcal{T},\emptyset,q_{\exists r.C})(a)
. By Lemma
19
, this implies
that there is some
m\geq 0
with
\mathcal{T}\models C_{a}^{m}\sqsubseteq\exists r.C
, where
C_{a}^{m}
is the unfolding of
\mathcal{A}
at
a
of depth
m
.
We show that this contradicts the assumption that
(\mathcal{T},\Sigma_{\mathsf{C}})
is safe.
There does not exist a tlc
\exists r.C^{\prime}
of
C_{a}^{m}
with

\mathcal{T}\models C^{\prime}\sqsubseteq C
because otherwise there is some

b\in\mathsf{Ind}(\mathcal{A})
with
r(a,b)\in\mathcal{A}
and
\mathcal{A}\models(\mathcal{T},\emptyset,q_{C})(b)
and thus,
a\cdot r\cdot C
would have
never been introduced by Rule (R3) in the construction of

\mathcal{J}_{\mathcal{T},\mathcal{A}}
. Moreover, there is no

\mathcal{EL}
concepts
E
with
{\sf sig}(E)\subseteq\Sigma_{\mathsf{C}}
and
\mathcal{T}\models C_{a}^{m}\sqsubseteq\exists r.E
and
\mathcal{T}\models E\sqsubseteq C
because
otherwise there is a
b\in\mathsf{Ind}(\mathcal{A})
with
r(a,b)\in\mathcal{A}
and

\mathcal{I}_{\mathcal{A}}\models E(b)
since
\mathcal{A}
is
consistent w.r.t.
(\mathcal{T},\Sigma_{\mathsf{C}})
. But then
\mathcal{A}\models(\mathcal{T},\emptyset,q_{C})(b)
by the fact that
\mathcal{J}_{\mathcal{T},\mathcal{A}}

is a model of
\mathcal{T}
and
\mathcal{A}
. Again, in this case,
a\cdot r\cdot C
would have never been introduced by Rule (R3) in the
construction of
\mathcal{J}_{\mathcal{T},\mathcal{A}}
.
Hence
C_{a}^{m}\sqsubseteq\exists r.C
witnesses that
(\mathcal{T},\Sigma_{\mathsf{C}})
is not safe.

For Item (4), assume for a proof by contradiction that there is a

d\in\Delta^{\mathcal{J}_{\mathcal{T},\mathcal{A}}}\setminus\mathsf{Ind}(\mathcal{A})
and an

\mathcal{EL}
concept
D
such that
d\in D^{\mathcal{J}_{\mathcal{T},\mathcal{A}}}
and

\mathsf{sig}(D)\cap\Sigma_{\mathsf{C}}\neq\emptyset
. By definition,
d=a\cdot r_{0}\cdot C_{0}\cdots r_{n}\cdot C_{n}
for some
a\in\mathsf{Ind}(\mathcal{A})
. Let

G=C_{0}\sqcap\exists r_{1}.\exists r_{2}\ldots\exists r_{n}.D
. Obviously,
\mathsf{sig}(G)\cap\Sigma_{\mathsf{C}}\neq\emptyset
and

a\cdot r_{0}\cdot C_{0}\in G^{\mathcal{J}_{\mathcal{T},\mathcal{A}}}
. By the
latter and Lemma
17
, we have
\mathcal{A}\models(\mathcal{T},\emptyset,q_{\exists r_{0}.G})(a)
. By Lemma
19
, this implies
that there is some
m\geq 0
with
\mathcal{T}\models C_{a}^{m}\sqsubseteq\exists r_{0}.G
. We show that it follows that
(\mathcal{T},\Sigma_{\mathsf{C}})
is not safe.
We have
\mathsf{sig}(G)\cap\Sigma_{\mathsf{C}}\neq\emptyset
and, by Item (3),

r_{0}\not\in\Sigma_{\mathsf{C}}
. To show that
(\mathcal{T},\Sigma_{\mathsf{C}})
is not safe it remains to show that there
is no tlc
\exists r_{0}.C^{\prime}
of
C_{a}^{m}
with
\mathcal{T}\models C^{\prime}\sqsubseteq G
. This is indeed the case because otherwise there
is some
b\in\mathsf{Ind}(\mathcal{A})
with
r_{0}(a,b)\in\mathcal{A}
and

\mathcal{A}\models(\mathcal{T},\emptyset,q_{C_{0}})(b)
and thus,
a\cdot r_{0}\cdot C_{0}
would have never been introduced by Rule (R3) in the construction of
\mathcal{J}_{\mathcal{T},\mathcal{A}}
.

This finishes the proof of Part (a) of Point (2) of Theorem
14
.
Before we prove Part (b) of Point (2) we show the following
observation of independent interest.

Lemma 26
.

Let
(\mathcal{T},\Sigma_{\mathsf{C}})
be a safe
\mathcal{EL}
TBox with closed predicates.
Then there exists an
\mathcal{EL}
TBox
\mathcal{T}^{\prime}

equivalent to
\mathcal{T}
such that for any
C\sqsubseteq D\in\mathcal{T}^{\prime}
,
{\sf sig}(D)\subseteq\Sigma_{\mathsf{C}}
or
{\sf sig}(D)\cap\Sigma_{\mathsf{C}}=\emptyset
.

Proof 5.14
.

We apply the following three rules exhaustively (and recursively) to
\mathcal{T}
:

•

replace any
C\sqsubseteq D_{1}\sqcap D_{2}
by
C\sqsubseteq D_{1}
and
C\sqsubseteq D_{2}
;

•

replace any
C\sqsubseteq\exists r.D
such that there exists a tlc
\exists r.C^{\prime}
of

C
with
\mathcal{T}\models C^{\prime}\sqsubseteq D
by
C^{\prime}\sqsubseteq D
;

•

replace any
C\sqsubseteq\exists r.D
with
r\in\Sigma_{\mathsf{C}}
and
{\sf sig}(D)\not\subseteq\Sigma_{\mathsf{C}}
by
C\sqsubseteq\exists r.F
and

F\sqsubseteq D
, where
F
is an

\mathcal{EL}
concepts with
{\sf sig}(F)\subseteq\Sigma_{\mathsf{C}}
such that
\mathcal{T}\models C\sqsubseteq\exists r.F
and

\mathcal{T}\models F\sqsubseteq D
. (Note that such a concept
F
always exists by Condition
3(s2)
.)

It is straightforward to show that the resulting TBox
\mathcal{T}^{\prime}
is as required.

Recall that UCQ evaluation for
\mathcal{EL}
TBoxes without closed predicates is in
PTime
.
Thus, the following lemma and Part (a) directly imply Part (b) of Theorem
14
.

Lemma 27
.

Let
(\mathcal{T},\Sigma_{\mathsf{C}})
be a safe
\mathcal{EL}
TBox with closed predicates.
Then consistency of ABoxes w.r.t.
(\mathcal{T},\Sigma_{\mathsf{C}})
is in
PTime
.

Proof 5.15
.

We may assume that
\mathcal{T}
is in the form of the claim above: for all
C\sqsubseteq D\in\mathcal{T}
,
{\sf sig}(D)\subseteq\Sigma_{\mathsf{C}}
or
{\sf sig}(D)\cap\Sigma_{\mathsf{C}}=\emptyset
. We show that the following conditions are
equivalent, for every ABox
\mathcal{A}
:

(1)

\mathcal{A}
is consistent w.r.t.
(\mathcal{T},\Sigma_{\mathsf{C}})
;

(2)

for all
C\sqsubseteq F\in\mathcal{T}
with
{\sf sig}(F)\subseteq\Sigma_{\mathsf{C}}
and all
a\in{\sf Ind}(\mathcal{A})
, if
\mathcal{A}\models(\mathcal{T},\emptyset,q_{F})(a)
, then

\mathcal{I}_{\mathcal{A}}\models F(a)
.

The implication from Condition (1) to Condition (2) is obvious. Conversely, assume that Condition (2) holds.
It suffices to show that the tree-shaped canonical model
\mathcal{J}_{\mathcal{T},\mathcal{A}}
respects closed predicates
\Sigma_{\mathsf{C}}
.
One can readily check that the proofs of Points (2) and (4) of Lemma
25
do not use
the condition that
\mathcal{A}
is consistent
w.r.t.
(\mathcal{T},\Sigma_{\mathsf{C}})
. Thus, it suffices to prove that Points (1) and (3) of Lemma
25

hold for
\mathcal{J}_{\mathcal{T},\mathcal{A}}
.
But they follow directly from Condition (2) and the construction of
\mathcal{J}_{\mathcal{T},\mathcal{A}}
.
The result now follows from the fact that Condition (2) can be checked in polynomial time in the size of
\mathcal{A}
.

This finishes the proof of Theorem
14
.

6.
Quantified Query Case: Deciding Tractability of
PTime
Query Evaluation

We consider the meta problem to decide whether query evaluation w.r.t. a TBox with closed predicates is tractable.
We show that the following problems are in
PTime
:

(1)

decide whether UCQ evaluation w.r.t.
\text{DL-Lite}_{\mathcal{R}}
TBoxes with closed predicates is FO-rewritable (equivalently, in
PTime
); and

(2)

decide whether UCQ evaluation w.r.t.
\mathcal{EL}
TBoxes with closed predicates is in
PTime
.

In both cases, we use the characterization via safeness given in the previous section and show that safeness can be decided in
PTime
.
For
\text{DL-Lite}_{\mathcal{R}}
, the proof is actually straightforward: to check safeness of a
\text{DL-Lite}_{\mathcal{R}}
TBox with closed predicates
(\mathcal{T},\Sigma_{\mathsf{C}})

it suffices to consider all basic concepts
B_{1},B_{2}
and roles
r
from
{\sf sig}(\mathcal{T})
(of which there are only polynomially many)
and make satisfiability checks for basic concepts w.r.t.
\text{DL-Lite}_{\mathcal{R}}
TBoxes and entailment checks of
\text{DL-Lite}_{\text{core}}
CIs and RIs by
\text{DL-Lite}_{\mathcal{R}}
TBoxes
according to the definition of safeness. Both can be done in polynomial time
[
20
]
.

Theorem 28
.

It is in
PTime
to decide whether a
\text{DL-Lite}_{\mathcal{R}}
TBox with closed predicates is safe.

Such a straightforward argument does not work for
\mathcal{EL}
TBoxes
since Definition
5.3
quantifies over all
\mathcal{EL}
concepts
C
,

D
, and
E
, of which there are infinitely many. In the following, we
show that, nevertheless, safeness of an
\mathcal{EL}
TBox with closed
predicates
(\mathcal{T},\Sigma_{\mathsf{C}})
can be decided in
PTime
. The first step of the proof is
to convert
\mathcal{T}
into a
reduced

\mathcal{EL}
TBox
\mathcal{T}^{*}
with the following properties:

(red1)

\mathcal{T}^{*}
contains no CI of the form
C\sqsubseteq D_{1}\sqcap D_{2}
;

(red2)

if
C\sqsubseteq\exists r.D\in\mathcal{T}^{*}
,
then there is no tlc
\exists r.C^{\prime}
of
C
with
\mathcal{T}^{*}\models C^{\prime}\sqsubseteq D
.

Lemma 29
.

For every
\mathcal{EL}
TBox, one can compute in polynomial time an equivalent reduced
\mathcal{EL}
TBox.

Proof 6.1
.

Assume that
\mathcal{T}
is an
\mathcal{EL}
TBox. Compute
\mathcal{T}^{*}
by applying the following two rules exhaustively to
\mathcal{T}
:

•

replace any CI
C\sqsubseteq D_{1}\sqcap D_{2}
with the CIs
C\sqsubseteq D_{1}

and
C\sqsubseteq D_{2}
;

•

replace any CI
C\sqsubseteq\exists r.D
for which there exists a tlc
\exists r.C^{\prime}
of
C
with
\mathcal{T}\models C^{\prime}\sqsubseteq D

by
C^{\prime}\sqsubseteq D
.

It is straightforward to prove that
\mathcal{T}^{\ast}
is reduced, equivalent to
\mathcal{T}
, and is constructed in polynomial time
(using the fact that checking
\mathcal{T}\models C\sqsubseteq D
is in
PTime

[
7
]
).

We now formulate a stronger version of safeness. While
Definition
5.3
quantifies over all CIs

C\sqsubseteq\exists r.D
that are
entailed
by the TBox

\mathcal{T}
, the stronger version only considers CIs of this form that are

contained
in
\mathcal{T}
. For deciding tractability based on safeness,
this is clearly a drastic improvement since only the concept
E

from Definition
5.3
remains universally quantified.

{defi}

An
\mathcal{EL}
TBox with closed predicates
(\mathcal{T},\Sigma_{\mathsf{C}})
is

strongly safe
if there exists no
\mathcal{EL}
CI
C\sqsubseteq\exists r.D\in\mathcal{T}
such
that one of the following holds:

(st1)

r\not\in\Sigma_{\mathsf{C}}
and there is some
\mathcal{EL}
concept

E
such that
\mathcal{T}\models D\sqsubseteq E
and
{\sf sig}(E)\cap\Sigma_{\mathsf{C}}\not=\emptyset
;

(st2)

r\in\Sigma_{\mathsf{C}}
,
{\sf sig}(D)\not\subseteq\Sigma_{\mathsf{C}}
, and there is no
\mathcal{EL}
concept
E
with
{\sf sig}(E)\subseteq\Sigma_{\mathsf{C}}
such that

\mathcal{T}\models C\sqsubseteq\exists r.E
and
\mathcal{T}\models E\sqsubseteq D
.
\triangle

The crucial observation is that, for
\mathcal{EL}
TBoxes in reduced form, the
original notion of safeness can be replaced by strong safeness.

Lemma 30
.

If
\mathcal{T}
is a reduced
\mathcal{EL}
TBox and
\Sigma_{\mathsf{C}}
a signature, then
(\mathcal{T},\Sigma_{\mathsf{C}})
is safe iff it is strongly safe.

Proof 6.2
.

Suppose that
\mathcal{T}
satisfies Conditions
(red1)

and
(red2)
for reduced
\mathcal{EL}
TBoxes.

(\Rightarrow)
Suppose that
(\mathcal{T},\Sigma_{\mathsf{C}})
is not
strongly safe, that is, there is some

C\sqsubseteq\exists r.D\in\mathcal{T}
satisfying
(st1)
or

(st2)
. If
C\sqsubseteq\exists r.D
satisfies
Condition
(st1)
, then
r\not\in\Sigma_{\mathsf{C}}
and there is some
concept
E
such that
\mathcal{T}\models D\sqsubseteq E
and
{\sf sig}(E)\cap\Sigma_{\mathsf{C}}\not=\emptyset
. We show that
(\mathcal{T},\Sigma_{\mathsf{C}})
is not safe because the CI
C\sqsubseteq\exists r.(D\sqcap E)

violates safeness:

(1)

\mathcal{T}\models C\sqsubseteq\exists r.(D\sqcap E)
since

C\sqsubseteq\exists r.D\in\mathcal{T}
and
\mathcal{T}\models D\sqsubseteq E
.

(2)

there is no tlc
\exists r.C^{\prime}
of
C
with
\mathcal{T}\models C^{\prime}\sqsubseteq D\sqcap E
; this follows from Condition
(red2)
.

(3)

Condition
3(s1)
is satisfied because
r\notin\Sigma_{\mathsf{C}}
and

\mathsf{sig}(D\sqcap E)\cap\Sigma_{\mathsf{C}}\neq\emptyset
since
\mathsf{sig}(E)\cap\Sigma_{\mathsf{C}}\neq\emptyset
.

If
C\sqsubseteq\exists r.D
satisfies
Condition
(st2)
, then it follows directly that
(\mathcal{T},\Sigma_{\mathsf{C}})
is not safe because
C\sqsubseteq\exists r.D
satisfies
Condition
3(s2)
.

(
\Leftarrow
) Suppose that
(\mathcal{T},\Sigma_{\mathsf{C}})
is not safe. Take any
\mathcal{EL}
CI

C\sqsubseteq\exists r.D
violating safeness. In the following, we
use the tree-shaped canonical model
\mathcal{J}_{\mathcal{T},C}
defined above. For the sake of
readability denote the individual name
p
of
\mathcal{J}_{\mathcal{T},C}
by
a_{p}
(in particular,
a_{C}

denotes
C
). Note that Lemma
18
yields

a_{C}\in(\exists r.D)^{\mathcal{J}_{\mathcal{T},C}}
since
\mathcal{T}\models C\sqsubseteq\exists r.D
.
Thus there is some
d\in\Delta^{\mathcal{J}_{\mathcal{T},C}}
such that

(a_{C},d)\in r^{\mathcal{J}_{\mathcal{T},C}}
and

d\in D^{\mathcal{J}_{\mathcal{T},C}}
. By definition of

\mathcal{J}_{\mathcal{T},C}
,
d=a_{C\cdot r\cdot E}
for some
\mathcal{EL}
concept

E
. By Lemma
18
and

d\in D^{\mathcal{J}_{\mathcal{T},C}}
, we have
\mathcal{T}\models E\sqsubseteq D
.

Let
\mathcal{A}_{C}=\mathcal{A}_{0},\mathcal{A}_{1},\dots
be the ABoxes used in the
construction of
\mathcal{J}_{\mathcal{T},C}
. By definition of
\mathcal{A}_{C}
and
Condition
(red2)
, we have
C\cdot r\cdot E\not\in\mathsf{paths}(C)
, that is,

d=a_{C\cdot r\cdot E}
must have been generated by (R3).
Consequently, there is an
i\in\mathbb{N}
such that

\exists r.E(a_{C})\in\mathcal{A}_{i}
, and
\mathcal{A}_{i+1}=\mathcal{A}_{i}\cup\{r(a_{C},a_{C\cdot r\cdot E}),E(a_{C\cdot r\cdot E})\}
.
Using Condition
(red1)
one can now easily prove that

\exists r.E(a_{C})
can only have been added due to an application
of (R2).

Thus, there is some
C^{\prime}\sqsubseteq\exists r.E\in\mathcal{T}
with
\mathcal{I}_{\mathcal{A}_{j}}\models C^{\prime}(a_{C})
.
We obtain
a_{C}\in(C^{\prime})^{\mathcal{J}_{\mathcal{T},C}}
and this implies, by Lemma
18
,
that
\mathcal{T}\models C\sqsubseteq C^{\prime}
.
As
(\mathcal{T},\Sigma_{\mathsf{C}})
is not safe due to the CI

C\sqsubseteq\exists r.D
, we obtain one of the following cases:

•

C\sqsubseteq\exists r.D
satisfies
Condition
3(s1)
.
Then
r\not\in\Sigma_{\mathsf{C}}
and
\mathsf{sig}(D)\cap\Sigma_{\mathsf{C}}\neq\emptyset
. Since

\mathcal{T}\models E\sqsubseteq D
, we thus have that
C^{\prime}\sqsubseteq\exists r.E\in\mathcal{T}
satisfies Condition
(st1)
. We have shown that
\mathcal{T}
is not strongly safe.

•

C\sqsubseteq\exists r.D
satisfies
Condition
3(s2)
.
Then
r\in\Sigma_{\mathsf{C}}
,
\mathsf{sig}(D)\not\subseteq\Sigma_{\mathsf{C}}
, and there is no
\mathcal{EL}
concept
F
with
{\sf sig}(F)\subseteq\Sigma_{\mathsf{C}}
and

\mathcal{T}\models C\sqsubseteq\exists r.F
and
\mathcal{T}\models F\sqsubseteq D
.
We aim at showing that
C^{\prime}\sqsubseteq\exists r.E\in\mathcal{T}

satisfies Condition
(st2)
. We already know that
r\in\Sigma_{\mathsf{C}}
. From
\mathcal{T}\models C\sqsubseteq C^{\prime}\sqsubseteq\exists r.E
and
\mathcal{T}\models E\sqsubseteq D
, we obtain
\mathsf{sig}(E)\not\subseteq\Sigma_{\mathsf{C}}
(otherwise set
F:=E
above to derive a contradiction).
We also obtain that there is no
\mathcal{EL}
concept
F
with
{\sf sig}(F)\subseteq\Sigma_{\mathsf{C}}
and with
\mathcal{T}\models C^{\prime}\sqsubseteq\exists r.F
and
\mathcal{T}\models F\sqsubseteq E
. Again it follows that
\mathcal{T}
is not strongly safe.

Lemma 31
.

It is in
PTime
to decide whether a reduced
\mathcal{EL}
TBox with closed predicates is strongly safe.

Proof 6.3
.

Assume
(\mathcal{T},\Sigma_{\mathsf{C}})
is a reduced
\mathcal{EL}
TBox with closed predicates.
Assume
C\sqsubseteq\exists r.D\in\mathcal{T}
is given. It suffices to show that
Conditions
(st1)
and
(st2)
can be checked in polynomial time.
For Condition
(st1)
, it suffices to show that one can check in polynomial time whether there
exists an
\mathcal{EL}
concept
E
with
\mathcal{T}\models D\sqsubseteq E
and
{\sf sig}(E)\cap\Sigma_{\mathsf{C}}\not=\emptyset
.
We reduce this to a reachability problem in the directed graph induced by the finite canonical model
\mathcal{I}_{\mathcal{T},D}
.
In detail, let
G=(V,R)
be the directed graph
with
V=\Delta^{\mathcal{I}_{\mathcal{T},D}}
and
R=\bigcup_{r\in{\sf N_{R}}}r^{\mathcal{I}_{\mathcal{T},D}}
. Let
T=\bigcup_{A\in\Sigma_{\mathsf{C}}}A^{\mathcal{I}_{\mathcal{T},D}}\cup\bigcup_{r\in\Sigma_{\mathsf{C}}}(\exists r)^{\mathcal{I}_{\mathcal{T},D}}
.
Using Lemma
15
, it is readily checked that there exists an
\mathcal{EL}
concept
E

with
\mathcal{T}\models D\sqsubseteq E
and
{\sf sig}(E)\cap\Sigma_{\mathsf{C}}\not=\emptyset
iff
there exists a path from
a_{D}
to a node in
T
in
G
. The latter reachability problem can be checked in
polynomial time.

For Condition
(st2)
, assume
r\in\Sigma_{\mathsf{C}}
and let
\mathcal{A}
denote the ABox corresponding to
the
\Sigma
-reduct of
\mathcal{I}_{\mathcal{T},C}
. We show that the following conditions are equivalent:

(1)

there exists an
\mathcal{EL}
concept
E
such that
{\sf sig}(E)\subseteq\Sigma_{\mathsf{C}}
,
\mathcal{T}\models C\sqsubseteq\exists r.E

and
\mathcal{T}\models E\sqsubseteq D
;

(2)

there exists
a\in{\sf Ind}(\mathcal{A})
such that
(a_{C},a)\in r^{\mathcal{I}_{\mathcal{T},C}}
and
\mathcal{A}\models(\mathcal{T},\emptyset,q_{D})(a)
.

For the proof of the implication from (1) to (2) take an
\mathcal{EL}
concept
E
satisfying (1). Then
a_{C}\in(\exists r.E)^{\mathcal{I}_{\mathcal{T},C}}
,
by Lemma
15
. Then there exists
a\in{\sf Ind}(\mathcal{A})
such that
(a_{C},a)\in r^{\mathcal{I}_{\mathcal{T},C}}
and
a\in E^{\mathcal{I}_{\mathcal{T},C}}
.
Hence
a\in E^{\mathcal{I}_{\mathcal{A}}}
as
{\sf sig}(E)\subseteq\Sigma_{\mathsf{C}}
. There is an unfolding
C_{a}^{m}
of
\mathcal{A}
at
a
of depth
m
such that

\emptyset\models C_{a}^{m}\sqsubseteq E
.
From
\mathcal{T}\models E\sqsubseteq D
we obtain
\mathcal{T}\models C_{a}^{m}\sqsubseteq D
.
But then, by Lemma
19
,
\mathcal{A}\models(\mathcal{T},\emptyset,q_{D})(a)
, as required.

Conversely, let
a\in{\sf Ind}(\mathcal{A})
such that
(a_{C},a)\in r^{\mathcal{I}_{\mathcal{T},C}}
and
\mathcal{A}\models(\mathcal{T},\emptyset,q_{D})(a)
.
By Lemma
19
, there exists an unfolding
C_{a}^{m}
of
\mathcal{A}
at
a
such that
\mathcal{T}\models C_{a}^{m}\sqsubseteq D
.
It is readily checked that
E=C_{d}^{m}
is as required for Condition (1).

Condition (2) can be checked in
PTime
since query evaluaton for OMQCs in
(\mathcal{EL},\emptyset,\text{dtCQ})
is in
PTime
(in combined complexity).

Theorem 32
.

It is in
PTime
to decide whether an
\mathcal{EL}
TBox with closed predicates is safe.

Proof 6.4
.

Assume
(\mathcal{T},\Sigma_{\mathsf{C}})
is given. By Lemma
29
, we can construct, in polynomial time,
a reduced
\mathcal{EL}
TBox
\mathcal{T}^{\prime}
equivalent to
\mathcal{T}
. By Lemma
31
, we can check in

PTime
whether
(\mathcal{T}^{\prime},\Sigma_{\mathsf{C}})
is strongly safe. By Lemma
30
, strong safeness is
equivalent to safeness for
(\mathcal{T}^{\prime},\Sigma_{\mathsf{C}})
.

7.
Closing Concept Names in the Fixed Query Case and Surjective CSPs

We now switch from the quantified query case to the fixed query case.
In this section, we consider OMQC languages that only admit closing
concept names while the case of closing role names is deferred to the
subsequent section. Regarding the former, our main aim is to establish
a close connection between UCQ evaluation for such OMQC languages and
generalized surjective constraint satisfaction problems (CSPs). Let
BUtCQ denote the class of Boolean queries that can be obtained from a
union of tCQs by existentially quantifying the answer variable and let
BAQ denote the class of
Boolean atomic queries
which take the
form
\exists xA(x)
,
A
a concept name. We consider OMQC languages
between
(\text{DL-Lite}_{\text{core}},{\sf N_{C}},\text{BUtCQ})
and

(\mathcal{ALC}\mathcal{HI},{\sf N_{C}},\text{BUtCQ})
as well as between

(\mathcal{EL},{\sf N_{C}},\text{BUtCQ})
and
(\mathcal{ALC}\mathcal{HI},{\sf N_{C}},\text{BUtCQ})
and show that
for all these, a
PTime
/
coNP
dichotomy is equivalent to a
PTime
/
NP
dichotomy for generalized surjective CSPs, a problem that is
wide open. In fact, understanding the complexity of surjective CSPs,
generalized or not, is a very difficult, ongoing research effort. As
pointed out in the introduction, there are even concrete surjective
CSPs with very few elements whose complexity is unknown and, via the
connection established in this section, these problems can be used to
derive concrete OMQCs from the mentioned languages whose computational
properties are currently not understood.

We next introduce CSPs and then give a more detailed overview of the
results obtained in this section. An interpretation
\mathcal{I}
is a

\Sigma
-interpretation
if it only interprets predicates in

\Sigma
, that is, all other predicates are interpreted as empty. For
every finite
\Sigma
-interpretation
\mathcal{I}
we denote by

\mathsf{CSP}(\mathcal{I})
the following
constraint satisfaction problem
(in signature
\Sigma
)
: given a finite

\Sigma
-interpretation
\mathcal{J}
, decide whether there is a homomorphism

h
from
\mathcal{J}
to
\mathcal{I}
. The
surjective constraint
satisfaction problem
,
\mathsf{CSP}(\mathcal{I})^{\mathsf{sur}}
, is the variant
of
\mathsf{CSP}(\mathcal{I})
where we require
h
to be surjective.
\mathcal{I}
is
then called the
template
of
\mathsf{CSP}(\mathcal{I})^{\mathsf{sur}}
. In
this article we only consider CSPs with predicates of arity at most two.
A
generalized
surjective CSP in signature
\Sigma
is characterized by a

finite set

\Gamma
of finite
\Sigma
-interpretations instead
of a single such interpretation, denoted

\mathsf{CSP}(\Gamma)^{\mathsf{sur}}
. The problem is to decide, given a

\Sigma
-interpretation
\mathcal{J}
, whether there is a surjective
homomorphism from
\mathcal{J}
to some interpretation in
\Gamma
. The
interpretations
\mathcal{I}
in
\Gamma
are called the
templates
of

\mathsf{CSP}(\Gamma)^{\mathsf{sur}}
.

We first show that for every constraint satisfaction problem
\mathsf{CSP}(\Gamma)^{\mathsf{sur}}
, there is an
OMQC
Q
from
(\text{DL-Lite}_{\text{core}},{\sf N_{C}},\text{BUtCQ})
such that the
evaluation problem for
Q
has the same complexity as the complement
of
\mathsf{CSP}(\Gamma)^{\mathsf{sur}}
, up to polynomial time reductions; we
then observe that the same holds for
(\mathcal{EL},{\sf N_{C}},\text{BAQ})
.
To achieve a cleaner presentation, we first present the construction
for non-generalized surjective CSPs and then sketch the modifications
required to lift it it to generalized surjective CSPs.
Consider
\mathsf{CSP}(\mathcal{I})^{\mathsf{sur}}
in signature
\Sigma
. Let
A
,

V
, and
V_{d}
,
d\in\Delta^{\mathcal{I}}
, be concept names not in

\Sigma
, and
{\sf val}
,
{\sf aux}_{d}
, and
{\sf force}_{d}
,

d\in\Delta^{\mathcal{I}}
, be role names not in
\Sigma
. Define the OMQC

Q_{\mathcal{I}}=(\mathcal{T},\Sigma_{\mathsf{A}},\Sigma_{\mathsf{C}},q)
from

(\text{DL-Lite}_{\text{core}},{\sf N_{C}},\text{BUtCQ})
as follows:

\begin{array}[]{rcl}\mathcal{T}&=&\{A\sqsubseteq\exists\mathsf{val},\ \exists\mathsf{val}^{-}\sqsubseteq V\}\,\cup\\[2.84526pt]
&&\{A\sqsubseteq\exists\mathsf{aux}_{d},\ \exists\mathsf{aux}_{d}^{-}\sqsubseteq V\sqcap V_{d}\mid d\in\Delta^{\mathcal{I}}\}\,\cup\\[2.84526pt]
&&\{A\sqsubseteq\exists\mathsf{force}_{d},\ \exists\mathsf{force}^{-}_{d}\sqsubseteq A\mid d\in\Delta^{\mathcal{I}}\}\\[5.69054pt]
\Sigma_{\mathsf{C}}&=&\{A,V\}\cup\{V_{d}\mid d\in\Delta^{\mathcal{I}}\}\\[5.69054pt]
\Sigma_{\mathsf{A}}&=&\Sigma\cup\Sigma_{\mathsf{C}}\\[5.69054pt]
q&=&q_{1}\lor q_{2}\lor q_{3}\lor q_{4}\end{array}

where

\begin{array}[]{r@{\,}c@{\,}l}q_{1}&=&\,\,\,\,\displaystyle\bigvee_{d,d^{\prime}\in\Delta^{\mathcal{I}}\mid d\neq d^{\prime}}\exists x\exists y_{1}\exists y_{2}\,A(x)\wedge\mathsf{val}(x,y_{1})\;\wedge\\[-8.53581pt]
&&\hskip 91.04872pt\mathsf{val}(x,y_{2})\wedge V_{d}(y_{1})\wedge V_{d^{\prime}}(y_{2})\\[2.84526pt]
q_{2}&=&\displaystyle\bigvee_{d\in\Delta^{\mathcal{I}},E\in\Sigma\mid d\not\in E^{\mathcal{I}}}\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\exists x\exists y\,A(x)\wedge E(x)\;\wedge\\[-8.53581pt]
&&\hskip 91.04872pt\mathsf{val}(x,y)\wedge V_{d}(y)\\[2.84526pt]
q_{3}&=&\!\!\!\!\!\displaystyle\bigvee_{d,d^{\prime}\in\Delta^{\mathcal{I}},r\in\Sigma\mid(d,d^{\prime})\notin r^{\mathcal{I}}}\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\exists x\exists y\exists x_{1}\exists y_{1}\,A(x)\wedge A(y)\wedge r(x,y)\;\wedge\\[-8.53581pt]
&&\hskip 91.04872pt\mathsf{val}(x,x_{1})\wedge\mathsf{val}(y,y_{1})\;\wedge\\[4.2679pt]
&&\hskip 91.04872ptV_{d}(x_{1})\wedge V_{d^{\prime}}(y_{1})\\[2.84526pt]
q_{4}&=&\,\,\,\,\displaystyle\bigvee_{d,d^{\prime}\in\Delta^{\mathcal{I}}\mid d\neq d^{\prime}}\exists x\exists y\exists z\,A(x)\wedge\mathsf{force}_{d}(z,x)\;\wedge\\[-8.53581pt]
&&\hskip 91.04872pt\mathsf{val}(x,y)\wedge V_{d^{\prime}}(y).\end{array}

The following lemma links
\mathsf{CSP}(\mathcal{I})^{\mathsf{sur}}
to the
constructed OMQC
Q_{\mathcal{I}}
.

Lemma 33
.

The complement of
\mathsf{CSP}(\mathcal{I})^{\mathsf{sur}}
and the evaluation problem for
Q_{\mathcal{I}}

are polynomially reducible to each other.

Proof 7.1
.

Assume that
\mathsf{CSP}(\mathcal{I})^{\mathsf{sur}}
is given.
For the polynomial reduction of
\mathsf{CSP}(\mathcal{I})^{\mathsf{sur}}
to the evaluation problem for
Q_{\mathcal{I}}
,
let
\mathcal{J}
be a
\Sigma
-interpretation that is an input of

\mathsf{CSP}(\mathcal{I})^{\mathsf{sur}}
. Let
\mathcal{A}_{\mathcal{J}}
be the ABox corresponding to
\mathcal{J}
. Introduce, for every
d\in\Delta^{\mathcal{I}}
, a fresh individual name
a_{d}
and let
the ABox
\mathcal{A}
be defined as

\mathcal{A}_{\mathcal{J}}\cup\{A(a_{d})\mid d\in\Delta^{\mathcal{J}}\}\cup\{V(a_{d}),V_{d}(a_{d})\mid d\in\Delta^{\mathcal{I}}\}.

Obviously,
\mathcal{A}
can be constructed in polynomial time. We claim
that
\mathcal{J}\in\mathsf{CSP}(\mathcal{I})^{\mathsf{sur}}
iff
\mathcal{A}\not\models Q_{\mathcal{I}}
.

(\Rightarrow)
Suppose that there is a surjective homomorphism
h

from
\mathcal{J}
to
\mathcal{I}
. Define the interpretation
\mathcal{I}^{\prime}
as follows:

\displaystyle\Delta^{\mathcal{I}^{\prime}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\mathsf{Ind}(\mathcal{A})

\displaystyle A^{\mathcal{I}^{\prime}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\mathsf{Ind}(\mathcal{A}_{\mathcal{J}})

\displaystyle V^{\mathcal{I}^{\prime}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\Delta^{\mathcal{I}}

\displaystyle V_{d}^{\mathcal{I}^{\prime}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\{a_{d}\},\text{ for all }d\in\Delta^{\mathcal{I}}

\displaystyle\mathsf{val}^{\mathcal{I}^{\prime}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\{(a,a_{h(a)})\mid a\in\mathsf{Ind}(\mathcal{A}_{\mathcal{J}})\}

\displaystyle\mathsf{aux}_{d}^{\mathcal{I}^{\prime}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\{(a,a_{d})\mid a\in\mathsf{Ind}(\mathcal{A}_{\mathcal{J}})\}\text{, for all }d\in\Delta^{\mathcal{I}}

\displaystyle\mathsf{force}_{d}^{\mathcal{I}^{\prime}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\{(a,a^{\prime})\in\mathsf{Ind}(\mathcal{A}_{\mathcal{J}})\times\mathsf{Ind}(\mathcal{A}_{\mathcal{J}})\mid h(a^{\prime})=d\}\text{, for all }d\in\Delta^{\mathcal{I}}

\displaystyle P^{\mathcal{I}^{\prime}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle P^{\mathcal{J}}\text{, for all predicates }P\not\in(\{A,V,\mathsf{val}\}\cup\{V_{d},\mathsf{aux}_{d},\mathsf{force}_{d}\mid d\in\Delta^{\mathcal{I}}\})

One can now verify that
\mathcal{I}^{\prime}
is a model of
\mathcal{T}
and
\mathcal{A}
that
respects closed predicates
\Sigma_{\mathsf{C}}
, and that

\mathcal{I}^{\prime}\not\models q
. Thus,
\mathcal{A}\not\models Q_{\mathcal{I}}
, as required.

(\Leftarrow)
Suppose
\mathcal{A}\not\models Q_{\mathcal{I}}
. Then there is a model

\mathcal{I}^{\prime}
of
\mathcal{T}
and
\mathcal{A}
that respects closed predicates

\Sigma_{\mathsf{C}}
and such that
\mathcal{I}^{\prime}\not\models q
. Define

h=\{(d,a_{e})\in\mathsf{val}^{\mathcal{I}^{\prime}}\mid d\in\Delta^{\mathcal{J}}\}
. We show
that
h
is a surjective homomorphism from
\mathcal{J}
to
\mathcal{I}
.

We first show that the relation
h
is a function. Assume that this
is not the case, that is, there are
d\in\Delta^{\mathcal{J}}
and

e_{1},e_{2}\in\Delta^{\mathcal{I}}
such that
e_{1}\neq e_{2}
and
(d,a_{e_{i}})\in\mathsf{val}^{\mathcal{I}^{\prime}}
for
i\in\{1,2\}
. Note that
a_{e_{i}}\in V_{e_{i}}^{\mathcal{I}^{\prime}}
. Thus we get
\mathcal{I}^{\prime}\models q_{1}
, which is a
contradiction against our choice of
\mathcal{I}^{\prime}
.

To show that
h
is
total, take some
d\in\Delta^{\mathcal{J}}
. Then
d\in A^{\mathcal{I}^{\prime}}
and
thus the first line of
\mathcal{T}
yields an
f\in V^{\mathcal{J}}
with
(d,f)\in\mathsf{val}^{\mathcal{I}^{\prime}}
. Since
V
is closed, we must have
f=a_{e}
for
some
e
, and thus
h(a_{e})=f
.

We show that
h
is a homomorphism. We show, using
q_{3}
, that
h
preserves role names. Using
q_{2}
, one can show in
the same way that
h
preserves concept names. Assume for a
contradiction that there is
(d,e)\in r^{\mathcal{J}}
with

(h(d),h(e))\not\in r^{\mathcal{I}}
. The latter implies that
the following is a disjunct of
q_{3}
:

\begin{array}[]{l}\exists x\exists y\exists x_{1}\exists y_{1}\,A(x)\wedge A(y)\wedge r(x,y)\wedge\mathsf{val}(x,x_{1})\;\wedge\\[2.84526pt]
\hskip 56.9055pt\mathsf{val}(y,y_{1})\wedge V_{h(d)}(x_{1})\wedge V_{h(e)}(y_{1}).\end{array}

Note that
d,e\in A^{\mathcal{I}^{\prime}}
,
(d,a_{h(a)}),(e,a_{h(e)})\in\mathsf{val}^{\mathcal{I}^{\prime}}
,
a_{h(d)}\in V^{\mathcal{I}^{\prime}}_{h(d)}
, and
a_{h(e)}\in V^{\mathcal{I}^{\prime}}_{h(e)}
. Thus
\mathcal{I}^{\prime}\models q_{3}
, which contradicts our choice of
\mathcal{I}^{\prime}
.

It remains to show that
h
is surjective. Fix a
d\in\Delta^{\mathcal{I}}
. We
have to show that there is an
e\in\Delta^{\mathcal{J}}
with

h(e)=d
. Take some
f\in\Delta^{\mathcal{J}}
. Then by the third line of

\mathcal{T}
and since
A
is closed, there is some
e\in\Delta^{\mathcal{J}}
such
that
(f,e)\in\mathsf{force}_{d}^{\mathcal{I}^{\prime}}
. We show that
e
is as
required. Assume to the contrary that
h(e)\neq d
. Then the
following is a disjunct of
q_{4}
:

A(x)\wedge\mathsf{force}_{d}(z,x)\wedge\mathsf{val}(x,y)\wedge V_{h(e)}(y).

Note that
f\in A^{\mathcal{I}^{\prime}}
,
(e,a_{h(e)})\in\mathsf{val}^{\mathcal{I}^{\prime}}
,
and
a_{h(e)}\in V_{h(e)}^{\mathcal{I}^{\prime}}
. Thus,
\mathcal{I}^{\prime}\models q_{4}
which contradicts our choice of

\mathcal{I}^{\prime}
. This finishes the proof of the reduction from
\mathsf{CSP}(\mathcal{I})^{\mathsf{sur}}
to evaluating
Q_{\mathcal{I}}
.

We now give the polynomial reduction of the evaluation problem for
Q_{\mathcal{I}}
to
\mathsf{CSP}(\mathcal{I})^{\mathsf{sur}}
.
Assume a
\Sigma_{\mathsf{A}}
-ABox
\mathcal{A}
is given. To decide whether
\mathcal{A}\models Q_{\mathcal{I}}
, we start with the following:

(1)

If
\mathcal{A}
does not contain any assertion of the form
A(a)
, then
\mathcal{A}\not\models Q_{\mathcal{I}}
. In fact, let

\mathcal{I}_{\mathcal{A}}
be
\mathcal{A}
viewed as an interpretation.
Then
\mathcal{I}_{\mathcal{A}}
is a model of
\mathcal{A}
that respects closed predicates
\Sigma_{\mathsf{C}}
. Since
\mathcal{A}
does not contain any assertion of the form
A(a)
,
\mathcal{I}_{\mathcal{A}}
is also a model of
\mathcal{T}
and satisfies
\mathcal{I}_{\mathcal{A}}\not\models q
(note that each disjunct
of
q
demands the existence of an instance of
A
). Thus answer ‘
\mathcal{A}\not\models Q_{\mathcal{I}}
’.

(2)

Otherwise, if
\mathcal{A}
does not contain for each
d\in\Delta^{\mathcal{I}}
an individual name
a
with
V(a),V_{d}(a)\in\mathcal{A}
,
then
\mathcal{A}
is not consistent w.r.t.
(\mathcal{T},\Sigma_{\mathsf{C}})
. Thus
answer ‘
\mathcal{A}\models Q_{\mathcal{I}}
’.

(3)

Otherwise, if
\mathcal{A}
contains an individual name
a
with

V(a)\in\mathcal{A}
and
V_{d}(a)\not\in\mathcal{A}
for each
d\in\Delta^{\mathcal{I}}
, then
\mathcal{A}\not\models Q_{\mathcal{I}}
. In fact,
we can build a model of
\mathcal{A}
and
\mathcal{T}
that makes
q

false in the following way:
Line 1 of
\mathcal{T}
can be satisfied by linking every element to

a
via
\mathsf{val}
; Line 2 can be satisfied since Case (2)
above does not apply; Line 3 can trivially be satisfied. All
remaining choices can be taken in an arbitrary way.

If none of the above applies, let
\mathcal{A}_{|A}
be the restriction
of
\mathcal{A}
to
\{a\in{\sf Ind}(\mathcal{A})\mid A(a)\in\mathcal{A}\}
. Since Case (1)
above does not apply,
\mathcal{A}_{|A}
is non-empty. Let
\mathcal{J}_{A}
be the
\Sigma
-reduct of the
interpretation corresponding to
\mathcal{A}_{|A}
.
We show that
\mathcal{J}_{A}\in\mathsf{CSP}(\mathcal{I})^{\mathsf{sur}}
iff
\mathcal{A}\not\models Q_{\mathcal{I}}
.

(\Leftarrow)
. Assume that

\mathcal{A}\not\models Q_{\mathcal{I}}
. Then there is a model

\mathcal{J}
of
\mathcal{T}
and
\mathcal{A}
that respects closed predicates

\Sigma_{\mathsf{C}}
and such that
\mathcal{J}\not\models q
. By the first
line of
\mathcal{T}
, since
V
is closed, Case (3) does not apply, and by
q_{1}
, for each

a\in\mathsf{Ind}(\mathcal{A}_{|A})
there is
exactly one
d\in\Delta^{\mathcal{I}}
such that
a\in(\exists\mathsf{val}.V_{d})^{\mathcal{J}}
.
Define a homomorphism
h:\mathcal{J}_{A}\rightarrow\mathcal{I}
by
mapping each
a
in
\mathcal{A}_{|A}
to the value
d\in\Delta^{\mathcal{I}}
thus
determined. By
q_{2}
and
q_{3}
,
h
is indeed a
homomorphism. By the third line of
\mathcal{T}
and
q_{4}

and since
A
is closed,
h
must be surjective.

(\Rightarrow)
. Assume that
\mathcal{J}_{A}\in\mathsf{CSP}(\mathcal{I})^{\mathsf{sur}}
, and let
h
be a surjective homomorphism
from
\mathcal{J}_{A}
to
\mathcal{I}
. Build an interpretation
\mathcal{J}
as
follows. Start by setting
\mathcal{J}=\mathcal{I}_{\mathcal{A}}
. Since Case (2) above does
not apply, for each
d\in\Delta^{\mathcal{I}}
we can select an individual name

a_{d}
of
\mathcal{A}
such that
V(a_{d})
and
V_{d}(a_{d})
are in
\mathcal{A}
. For each
individual name
a
in
\mathcal{A}_{|A}
, extend
\mathcal{J}
by adding
(a,a_{h(a)})
to

\mathsf{val}^{\mathcal{J}}
and
(a,a_{d})
to
\mathsf{aux}_{d}^{\mathcal{J}}
for each
d\in\Delta^{\mathcal{I}}
. Since
h
is surjective, for each
d\in\Delta^{\mathcal{I}}

there must be an individual name
a_{d}^{\prime}
of
\mathcal{A}_{|A}
with
h(a_{d}^{\prime})=d
. Further
extend
\mathcal{J}
by adding
(a,a_{d}^{\prime})
to
\mathsf{force}_{d}^{\mathcal{J}}
for all
a\in\mathsf{Ind}(\mathcal{A}_{|A})
and all
d\in\Delta^{\mathcal{I}}
. It is readily
checked that
\mathcal{J}
is a model of
\mathcal{T}
and
\mathcal{A}
that respects
closed predicates
\Sigma_{\mathsf{C}}
, and that
\mathcal{J}\not\models q
. Thus,
\mathcal{A}\not\models Q_{\mathcal{I}}
, as required.

Note that the same reduction works when
\text{DL-Lite}_{\text{core}}
is
replaced with
\mathcal{EL}
. One simply has to replace the TBox
\mathcal{T}
by the
\mathcal{EL}
TBox

\begin{array}[]{rcl}\mathcal{T}^{\prime}&=&\{A\sqsubseteq\exists\mathsf{val}.V\}\,\cup\\[2.84526pt]
&&\{A\sqsubseteq\exists\mathsf{aux}_{d}.(V\sqcap V_{d})\mid d\in\Delta^{\mathcal{I}}\}\,\cup\\[2.84526pt]
&&\{A\sqsubseteq\exists\mathsf{force}_{d}.A\mid d\in\Delta^{\mathcal{I}}\}\end{array}

and observe that all CQs in
q
have the form
\exists xq^{\prime}(x)

with
q^{\prime}(x)
a dtCQ which enables the following modification:
introduce a fresh concept name
B
, then for each CQ
\exists xq^{\prime}(x)

in
q
, take the
\mathcal{EL}
concepts
C_{q^{\prime}}
that corresponds to
q^{\prime}(x)
and
extend
\mathcal{T}^{\prime}
with
C_{q^{\prime}}\sqsubseteq B
, and finally replace
q

with the BAQ
\exists x\,B(x)
.

We now describe how to extend the reduction from surjective CSPs to
generalized surjective CSPs. Let
\mathsf{CSP}(\Gamma)^{\mathsf{sur}}
be such a
CSP. Let
\Gamma=\{\mathcal{I}_{1},\dots,\mathcal{I}_{n}\}
. The main idea is to use

n
copies of each non-
\Sigma
symbol in the above reduction, one for
each template in
\Gamma
. Let the
i
-th copy of
A
be
A_{i}
, of

\mathsf{val}
be
\mathsf{val}_{i}
, and so on. This gives us
n
copies of the
TBox
\mathcal{T}
and the UCQ
q
in the above reduction, which we call

\mathcal{T}_{1},\dots,\mathcal{T}_{n}
and
q_{1},\dots,q_{n}
. Note that the
\mathcal{T}_{i}
do
